   330→    print(
   331→        colorize(
   332→            f"  1. Open launch prompt: `cat {shlex.quote(str(launch_prompt_path))}`",
   333→            "dim",
   334→        )
   335→    )
   336→    print(colorize(f"  2. Reviewer output target: `{output_path}`", "dim"))
   337→    print(colorize(f"  3. Submit results: `{submit_cmd}`", "dim"))
   338→    print(colorize(f"  4. Optional auto-rescan: `{submit_with_scan_cmd}`", "dim"))
   339→
   340→
   341→def _canonical_external_payload(
   342→    raw_payload: dict[str, Any],
   343→    *,
   344→    session: dict[str, Any],
   345→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   346→    """Return import payload with canonical provenance and required session token."""
   347→    session_meta = raw_payload.get("session")
   348→    if not isinstance(session_meta, dict):
   349→        raise CommandError(
   350→            "Error: external reviewer payload must include top-level `session` object."
   351→            ' Expected: {"session":{"id":"...","token":"..."},"assessments":{...},"issues":[...]}',
   352→        )
   353→
   354→    payload_id = str(session_meta.get("id", "")).strip()
   355→    payload_token = str(session_meta.get("token", "")).strip()
   356→    expected_id = str(session.get("session_id", "")).strip()
   357→    expected_token = str(session.get("token", "")).strip()
   358→    if payload_id != expected_id or payload_token != expected_token:
   359→        raise CommandError(
   360→            "Error: session id/token mismatch in external reviewer payload."
   361→            " Regenerate output using the session template/instructions.",
   362→        )
   363→
   364→    payload = {
   365→        key: value
   366→        for key, value in raw_payload.items()
   367→        if key not in {"session", "provenance"}
   368→    }
   369→    payload["provenance"] = {
   370→        "kind": "blind_review_batch_import",
   371→        "blind": True,
   372→        "runner": str(session.get("runner", "claude")),
   373→        "session_id": str(session.get("session_id", "")),
   374→        "created_at": _iso_seconds(_utc_now()),
   375→        "packet_path": str(session.get("blind_packet_path", "")),
   376→        "packet_sha256": str(session.get("packet_sha256", "")),
   377→    }
   378→    return payload
   379→
   380→
   381→def _ensure_session_open(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   382→    status = str(session.get("status", "open")).strip().lower()
   383→    if status == "open":
   384→        return
   385→    raise CommandError(
   386→        f"Error: session is not open (status={status or 'unknown'}). Start a new session with --external-start.",
   387→    )
   388→
   389→
   390→def _ensure_session_not_expired(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   391→    expires_at = _parse_iso(session.get("expires_at"))
   392→    if expires_at is None:
   393→        raise CommandError(
   394→            "Error: session metadata is missing/invalid expires_at.",
   395→        )
   396→    now = _utc_now()
   397→    if now <= expires_at:
   398→        return
   399→    raise CommandError(
   400→        f"Error: session expired at {session.get('expires_at')}. Start a new session with --external-start.",
   401→    )
   402→
   403→
   404→def do_external_submit(
   405→    *,
   406→    import_file: str,
   407→    session_id: str,
   408→    state: dict,
   409→    lang,
   410→    state_file,
   411→    config: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   412→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   413→    scan_after_import: bool = False,
   414→    scan_path: str = ".",
   415→    dry_run: bool = False,
   416→) -> None:
   417→    """Submit external reviewer output via session, adding canonical provenance."""
   418→    config = config or {}
   419→    session_path, session = _session_payload(session_id)
   420→    _ensure_session_open(session)
   421→    _ensure_session_not_expired(session)
   422→
   423→    if str(session.get("runner", "")).strip().lower() not in _EXTERNAL_SUPPORTED_RUNNERS:
   424→        raise CommandError(
   425→            "Error: only Claude external sessions currently support durable score submit.",
   426→        )
   427→
   428→    issues_path = Path(import_file)
   429→    raw_payload = _load_json_object(issues_path, label="external issues")
   430→    canonical_payload = _canonical_external_payload(raw_payload, session=session)
   431→
   432→    stamp = run_stamp()
   433→    session_dir = session_path.parent
   434→    canonical_path = session_dir / f"canonical_import_{stamp}.json"
   435→    safe_write_text(canonical_path, json.dumps(canonical_payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   436→
   437→    if dry_run:
   438→        do_validate_import(
   439→            str(canonical_path),
   440→            lang,
   441→            allow_partial=allow_partial,
   442→            attested_external=True,
   443→            manual_attest=str(session.get("attest", EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT)),
   444→        )
   445→        return
   446→
   447→    do_import(
   448→        str(canonical_path),
   449→        state,
   450→        lang,
   451→        state_file,
   452→        config=config,
   453→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   454→        attested_external=True,
   455→        manual_attest=str(session.get("attest", EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT)),
   456→    )
   457→
   458→    submitted_at = _iso_seconds(_utc_now())
   459→    session["status"] = "submitted"
   460→    session["submitted_at"] = submitted_at
   461→    session["submitted_input_file"] = str(issues_path)
   462→    session["submitted_canonical_file"] = str(canonical_path)
   463→    safe_write_text(session_path, json.dumps(session, indent=2) + "\n")
   464→
   465→    if scan_after_import:
   466→        code = run_followup_scan(
   467→            lang_name=lang.name,
   468→            scan_path=scan_path,
   469→            deps=FollowupScanDeps(
   470→                project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
   471→                timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   472→                python_executable=sys.executable,
   473→                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   474→                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   475→                colorize_fn=colorize,
   476→            ),
   477→        )
   478→        if code != 0:
   479→            raise SystemExit(code)
   480→
   481→
   482→__all__ = ["do_external_start", "do_external_submit", "EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT"]
   483→
```

1→"""Prompt template helpers for holistic review batch subagents."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (

    16→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    17→class _PromptBatchContext:
    18→    name: str
    19→    dimensions: tuple[str, ...]
    20→    rationale: str
    21→    seed_files: tuple[str, ...]
    22→    issues_cap: int
    23→
    24→    @property
    25→    def dimension_set(self) -> set[str]:
    26→        return set(self.dimensions)
    27→
    28→    @property
    29→    def dimensions_text(self) -> str:
    30→        return ", ".join(self.dimensions) if self.dimensions else "(none)"
    31→
    32→    @property

    34→        return "\n".join(f"- {path}" for path in self.seed_files) if self.seed_files else "- (none)"

    36→
    37→def _coerce_string_list(raw: object) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    38→    if not isinstance(raw, list | tuple):
    39→        return ()
    40→    return tuple(str(item) for item in raw if isinstance(item, str) and item)
    41→

    43→def _build_context(batch: dict[str, object], batch_index: int) -> _PromptBatchContext:
    44→    dimensions = _coerce_string_list(batch.get("dimensions", []))
    45→    return _PromptBatchContext(
    46→        name=str(batch.get("name", f"Batch {batch_index + 1}")),
    47→        dimensions=dimensions,
    48→        rationale=str(batch.get("why", "")).strip(),
    49→        seed_files=_coerce_string_list(batch.get("files_to_read", [])),
    50→        issues_cap=max_batch_issues_for_dimension_count(len(dimensions)),
    51→    )

    98→
    99→def _render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   100→    focus = batch.get("historical_issue_focus")
   101→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):
   102→        return ""
   103→
   104→    selected_raw = focus.get("selected_count", 0)
   105→    try:
   106→        selected_count = max(0, int(selected_raw))
   107→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   108→        selected_count = 0
   109→
   110→    issues = focus.get("issues", [])
   111→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
   112→        issues = []
   113→
   114→    if selected_count <= 0 or not issues:
   115→        return ""
   116→
   117→    lines: list[str] = []
   118→    lines.append(
   119→        "Previously flagged issues — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:"
   120→    )
   121→    lines.append(
   122→        "Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report"
   123→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"
   124→        " what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out."
   125→    )
   126→
   127→    for entry in issues:
   128→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   129→            continue
   130→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
   131→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   132→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
   133→
   134→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
   135→        if note:
   136→            line += f" (note: {note})"
   137→        lines.append(line)
   138→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   139→
   140→
   141→def _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   142→    """Render mechanically-generated concern hypotheses for this batch."""
   143→    signals = batch.get("concern_signals")
   144→    if not isinstance(signals, list) or not signals:
   145→        return ""
   146→
   147→    lines: list[str] = []
   148→    lines.append("Mechanical concern signals — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:")
   149→    lines.append(
   150→        "Confirm or refute each with your own code reading. Report only confirmed defects."
   151→    )
   152→
   153→    shown = 0
   154→    for entry in signals:
   155→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   156→            continue
   157→        file = str(entry.get("file", "")).strip() or "(unknown file)"
   158→        concern_type = str(entry.get("type", "")).strip() or "design_concern"
   159→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   160→        question = str(entry.get("question", "")).strip()
   161→        evidence_raw = entry.get("evidence", [])
   162→        evidence = (
   163→            [str(item).strip() for item in evidence_raw if isinstance(item, str) and item.strip()]
   164→            if isinstance(evidence_raw, list)
   165→            else []
   166→        )
   167→
   168→        lines.append(f"  - [{concern_type}] {file}")
   169→        if summary:
   170→            lines.append(f"    summary: {summary}")
   171→        if question:
   172→            lines.append(f"    question: {question}")
   173→        for snippet in evidence[:2]:
   174→            lines.append(f"    evidence: {snippet}")
   175→        shown += 1
   176→        if shown >= 8:
   177→            break
   178→
   179→    extra = max(0, len(signals) - shown)
   180→    if extra:
   181→        lines.append(f"  - (+{extra} more concern signals)")
   182→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   183→
   184→
   185→def _render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   186→    """Render workflow integrity checks for architecture/integration dimensions."""
   187→    if not dim_set.intersection(
   188→        {
   189→            "cross_module_architecture",
   190→            "high_level_elegance",
   191→            "mid_level_elegance",
   192→            "design_coherence",
   193→            "initialization_coupling",
   194→        }
   195→    ):
   196→        return ""
   197→    return (
   198→        "9j. Workflow integrity checks: when reviewing orchestration/queue/review flows,\n"
   199→        "    explicitly look for loop-prone patterns and blind spots:\n"
   200→        "    - repeated stale/reopen churn without clear exit criteria or gating,\n"
   201→        "    - packet/batch data being generated but dropped before prompt execution,\n"
   202→        "    - ranking/triage logic that can starve target-improving work,\n"
   203→        "    - reruns happening before existing open review work is drained.\n"
   204→        "    If found, propose concrete guardrails and where to implement them.\n"
   205→    )
   206→
   207→
   208→def _render_package_org_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   209→    if "package_organization" not in dim_set:
   210→        return ""
   211→    return (
   212→        "9a. For package_organization, ground scoring in objective structure signals from "
   213→        "`holistic_context.structure` (root_files fan_in/fan_out roles, directory_profiles, "
   214→        "coupling_matrix). Prefer thresholded evidence (for example: fan_in < 5 for root "
   215→        "stragglers, import-affinity > 60%, directories > 10 files with mixed concerns).\n"
   216→        "9b. Suggestions must include a staged reorg plan (target folders, move order, "
   217→        "and import-update/validation commands).\n"
   218→        "9c. Also consult `holistic_context.structure.flat_dir_issues` for directories "
   219→        "flagged as overloaded, fragmented, or thin-wrapper patterns.\n"
   220→    )
   221→
   222→
   223→def _render_abstraction_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   224→    if "abstraction_fitness" not in dim_set:
   225→        return ""
   226→    return (
   227→        "9d. For abstraction_fitness, use evidence from `holistic_context.abstractions`:\n"
   228→        "  - `delegation_heavy_classes`: classes where most methods forward to an inner "
   229→        "object — entries include class_name, delegate_target, sample_methods, and line number.\n"
   230→        "  - `facade_modules`: re-export-only modules with high re_export_ratio — entries "
   231→        "include samples (re-exported names) and loc.\n"
   232→        "  - `typed_dict_violations`: TypedDict fields accessed via .get()/.setdefault()/.pop() "
   233→        "— entries include typed_dict_name, violation_type, field, and line number.\n"
   234→        "  - `complexity_hotspots`: files where mechanical analysis found extreme parameter "
   235→        "counts, deep nesting, or disconnected responsibility clusters.\n"
   236→        "  Include `delegation_density`, `definition_directness`, and `type_discipline` "
   237→        "alongside existing sub-axes in dimension_notes when evidence supports it.\n"
   238→    )
   239→
   240→
   241→def _render_dimension_focus(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   242→    dim_set = context.dimension_set
   243→    return (
   244→        _render_package_org_focus(dim_set)
   245→        + _render_abstraction_focus(dim_set)
   246→        + _render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)
   247→        + _render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set)
   248→    )

   252→    *,
   253→    repo_root: Path,

   260→        f"Repository root: {repo_root}\n"

   262→        f"Batch index: {batch_index + 1}\n"

   268→
   269→def _render_scoring_frame() -> str:
   270→    return (
   271→        "YOUR TASK: Read the code for this batch's dimensions. For each dimension, judge "
   272→        "how well the codebase serves a developer from that perspective. The dimension "
   273→        "prompt in the blind packet defines what good looks like — that is your rubric. "
   274→        "Cite specific observations that explain your judgment.\n\n"
   275→        "WHAT WE GIVE YOU AND WHY: Below you will find automated scan evidence, historical "
   276→        "issues, and mechanical concern signals. These are navigation aids — starting points "
   277→        "for where to look. They are NOT evidence, NOT confirmed issues, and NOT inputs to your "
   278→        "scores. Only what you observe directly in the code informs your judgment.\n\n"

   281→
   282→def _render_scan_evidence_note() -> str:
   283→    return (
   284→        "Mechanical scan evidence — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:\n"
   285→        "The blind packet contains `holistic_context.scan_evidence` with aggregated signals "
   286→        "from all mechanical detectors — including complexity hotspots, error hotspots, signal "
   287→        "density index, boundary violations, and systemic patterns. Use these as starting "
   288→        "points for where to look beyond the seed files.\n\n"
   289→    )
   290→
   291→
   292→def _render_seed_files_block(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   293→    return f"Seed files (start here):\n{context.seed_files_text}\n\n"
   294→
   295→
   296→def _render_task_requirements(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   297→    return (
   298→        "Task requirements:\n"
   299→        "1. Read the blind packet and follow `system_prompt` constraints exactly.\n"
   300→        "1a. If previously flagged issues are listed above, use them as context for your review.\n"
   301→        "    Verify whether each still applies to the current code. Do not re-report fixed or\n"
   302→        "    wontfix issues. Use them as starting points to look deeper — inspect adjacent code\n"
   303→        "    and related modules for defects the prior review may have missed.\n"
   304→        "1b. If mechanical concern signals are listed above, confirm or refute each with your\n"
   305→        "    own code reading. Report only confirmed defects.\n"
   306→        "1c. Think structurally: when you spot multiple individual issues that share a common\n"
   307→        "    root cause (missing abstraction, duplicated pattern, inconsistent convention),\n"
   308→        "    explain the deeper structural issue in the issue, not just the surface symptom.\n"
   309→        "    If the pattern is significant enough, report the structural issue as its own issue\n"
   310→        "    with appropriate fix_scope ('multi_file_refactor' or 'architectural_change') and\n"
   311→        "    use `root_cause_cluster` to connect related symptom issues together.\n"
   312→        "2. Start with the seed files, then freely explore additional repository files likely to surface material issues.\n"
   313→        "2a. Prioritize high-signal leads: unexplored/lightly reviewed files, historical issue areas, and hotspot neighbors "
   314→        "(high coupling, god modules, large files, churn seams).\n"
   315→        "2b. Keep exploration targeted — follow strongest evidence paths first instead of attempting exhaustive coverage.\n"
   316→        "2c. Keep issues and scoring scoped to this batch's listed dimensions.\n"
   317→        "2d. Respect scope controls in the blind packet config: do not include files/directories marked by "
   318→        "`exclude`, `suppress`, or zone overrides that classify files as non-production (test/config/generated/vendor).\n"
   319→        f"3. Return 0-{context.issues_cap} high-quality issues for this batch (empty array allowed).\n"
   320→        "3a. Do not suppress real defects to keep scores high; report every material issue you can support with evidence.\n"
   321→        "3b. Do not default to 100. Reserve 100 for genuinely exemplary evidence in this batch.\n"
   322→        "4. Score/issue consistency is required: broader or more severe issues MUST lower dimension scores.\n"
   323→        f"4a. Any dimension scored below {LOW_SCORE_ISSUE_THRESHOLD:.1f} MUST include explicit feedback: add at least one "
   324→        "issue with the same `dimension` and a non-empty actionable `suggestion`.\n"
   325→        "5. Every issue must include `related_files` with at least 2 files when possible.\n"
   326→        "6. Every issue must include `dimension`, `identifier`, `summary`, `evidence`, `suggestion`, and `confidence`.\n"
   327→        "7. Every issue must include `impact_scope` and `fix_scope`.\n"
   328→        "8. Every scored dimension MUST include dimension_notes with concrete evidence.\n"
   329→        f"9. If a dimension score is >{HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD:.1f}, include `{DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY}` in dimension_notes.\n"
   330→        "10. Use exactly one decimal place for every assessment and abstraction sub-axis score.\n"
   331→        f"{_render_dimension_focus(context)}"
   332→        "11. Ignore prior chat context and any target-threshold assumptions.\n"
   333→        "12. Do not edit repository files.\n"
   334→        "13. Return ONLY valid JSON, no markdown fences.\n\n"
   335→    )
   336→
   337→
   338→def _render_scope_enums() -> str:
   339→    return (
   340→        "Scope enums:\n"
   341→        '- impact_scope: "local" | "module" | "subsystem" | "codebase"\n'
   342→        '- fix_scope: "single_edit" | "multi_file_refactor" | "architectural_change"\n\n'
   343→    )
   344→
   345→
   346→def _render_output_schema(context: _PromptBatchContext, batch_index: int) -> str:
   347→    return (
   348→        "Output schema:\n"
   349→        "{\n"
   350→        f'  "batch": "{context.name}",\n'
   351→        f'  "batch_index": {batch_index + 1},\n'
   352→        '  "assessments": {"<dimension>": <0-100 with one decimal place>},\n'
   353→        '  "dimension_notes": {\n'
   354→        '    "<dimension>": {\n'
   355→        '      "evidence": ["specific code observations"],\n'
   356→        '      "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   357→        '      "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   358→        '      "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   359→        f'      "{DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY}": "required when score >{HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD:.1f}",\n'
   360→        '      "sub_axes": {"abstraction_leverage": 0-100, "indirection_cost": 0-100, "interface_honesty": 0-100, "delegation_density": 0-100, "definition_directness": 0-100, "type_discipline": 0-100}  // required for abstraction_fitness when evidence supports it; all one decimal place\n'
   361→        "    }\n"
   362→        "  },\n"
   363→        '  "issues": [{\n'
   364→        '    "dimension": "<dimension>",\n'
   365→        '    "identifier": "short_id",\n'
   366→        '    "summary": "one-line defect summary",\n'
   367→        '    "related_files": ["relative/path.py"],\n'
   368→        '    "evidence": ["specific code observation"],\n'
   369→        '    "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",\n'
   370→        '    "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   371→        '    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   372→        '    "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   373→        '    "root_cause_cluster": "optional_cluster_name_when_supported_by_history"\n'
   374→        "  }],\n"
   375→        '  "retrospective": {\n'
   376→        '    "root_causes": ["optional: concise root-cause hypotheses"],\n'
   377→        '    "likely_symptoms": ["optional: identifiers that look symptom-level"],\n'
   378→        '    "possible_false_positives": ["optional: prior concept keys likely mis-scoped"]\n'
   379→        "  }\n"
   380→        "}\n"
   381→    )
   382→
   383→
   384→def _join_non_empty_sections(*sections: str) -> str:
   385→    return "".join(section for section in sections if section)
   386→
   387→
   388→def render_batch_prompt(
   389→    *,
   390→    repo_root: Path,
   391→    packet_path: Path,
   392→    batch_index: int,
   393→    batch: dict[str, object],
   394→) -> str:
   395→    """Render one subagent prompt for a holistic investigation batch."""
   396→    context = _build_context(batch, batch_index)
   397→    return _join_non_empty_sections(
   398→        _render_metadata_block(
   399→            repo_root=repo_root,
   400→            packet_path=packet_path,
   401→            batch_index=batch_index,
   402→            context=context,
   403→        ),
   404→        _render_scoring_frame(),
   405→        _render_scan_evidence_note(),
   406→        _render_seed_files_block(context),
   407→        _render_historical_focus(batch),
   408→        _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch),
   409→        _render_task_requirements(context),
   410→        _render_scope_enums(),
   411→        _render_output_schema(context, batch_index),
   412→    )
   413→
   414→
   415→__all__ = ["render_batch_prompt"]
   416→

143→def prepare_run_artifacts(
   144→    *,
   145→    stamp: str,
   146→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   147→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   148→    packet_path: Path,
   149→    run_root: Path,
   150→    repo_root: Path,
   151→    build_prompt_fn,
   152→    safe_write_text_fn,
   153→    colorize_fn,
   154→) -> tuple[Path, Path, dict[int, Path], dict[int, Path], dict[int, Path]]:
   155→    """Build prompt/output/log paths and persist prompts for selected batches."""
   156→    run_dir = run_root / stamp
   157→    prompts_dir = run_dir / "prompts"
   158→    results_dir = run_dir / "results"
   159→    logs_dir = run_dir / "logs"
   160→    prompts_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   161→    results_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   162→    logs_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   163→
   164→    selected_1_based = [idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes]
   165→    print(colorize_fn(f"\n  Running holistic batches: {selected_1_based}", "bold"))
   166→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Run artifacts: {run_dir}", "dim"))
   167→
   168→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   169→    output_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   170→    log_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   171→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   172→        batch = batches[idx] if isinstance(batches[idx], dict) else {}
   173→        prompt_text = build_prompt_fn(
   174→            repo_root=repo_root,
   175→            packet_path=packet_path,
   176→            batch_index=idx,
   177→            batch=batch,
   178→        )
   179→        prompt_file = prompts_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.md"
   180→        output_file = results_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.raw.txt"
   181→        log_file = logs_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.log"
   182→        safe_write_text_fn(prompt_file, prompt_text)
   183→        prompt_files[idx] = prompt_file
   184→        output_files[idx] = output_file
   185→        log_files[idx] = log_file
   186→    return run_dir, logs_dir, prompt_files, output_files, log_files
   187→

1→"""Subprocess-oriented runner helpers for review batch execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from ._runner_process_attempts import (
    10→    _handle_early_attempt_return,
    11→    _handle_failed_attempt,
    12→    _handle_successful_attempt,
    13→    _handle_timeout_or_stall,
    14→    _resolve_retry_config,
    15→    _run_batch_attempt,
    16→)
    17→from ._runner_process_io import _extract_payload_from_log  # noqa: F401 (runner_parallel import)
    18→from ._runner_process_types import (
    19→    CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    20→    FollowupScanDeps,
    21→)
    22→
    23→
    24→def codex_batch_command(*, prompt: str, repo_root: Path, output_file: Path) -> list[str]:
    25→    """Build one codex exec command line for a batch prompt."""
    26→    effort = os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT", "low").strip().lower()
    27→    if effort not in {"low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"}:
    28→        effort = "low"
    29→    return [
    30→        "codex",
    31→        "exec",
    32→        "--ephemeral",
    33→        "-C",
    34→        str(repo_root),
    35→        "-s",
    36→        "workspace-write",
    37→        "-c",
    38→        'approval_policy="never"',
    39→        "-c",
    40→        f'model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"',
    41→        "-o",
    42→        str(output_file),
    43→        prompt,
    44→    ]
    45→
    46→
    47→def run_codex_batch(
    48→    *,
    49→    prompt: str,
    50→    repo_root: Path,
    51→    output_file: Path,
    52→    log_file: Path,
    53→    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    54→    codex_batch_command_fn=None,
    55→) -> int:
    56→    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
    57→    if codex_batch_command_fn is None:
    58→        codex_batch_command_fn = codex_batch_command
    59→    cmd = codex_batch_command_fn(
    60→        prompt=prompt,
    61→        repo_root=repo_root,
    62→        output_file=output_file,
    63→    )
    64→    config = _resolve_retry_config(deps)
    65→    log_sections: list[str] = []
    66→
    67→    for attempt in range(1, config.max_attempts + 1):
    68→        header, result = _run_batch_attempt(
    69→            cmd=cmd,
    70→            deps=deps,
    71→            output_file=output_file,
    72→            log_file=log_file,
    73→            log_sections=log_sections,
    74→            attempt=attempt,
    75→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
    76→            use_popen=config.use_popen,
    77→            live_log_interval=config.live_log_interval,
    78→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    79→        )
    80→        early_return = _handle_early_attempt_return(result)
    81→        if early_return is not None:
    82→            return early_return
    83→        timeout_or_stall = _handle_timeout_or_stall(
    84→            header=header,
    85→            result=result,
    86→            deps=deps,
    87→            output_file=output_file,
    88→            log_file=log_file,
    89→            log_sections=log_sections,
    90→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    91→        )
    92→        if timeout_or_stall is not None:
    93→            return timeout_or_stall
    94→
    95→        log_sections.append(
    96→            f"{header}\n\nSTDOUT:\n{result.stdout_text}\n\nSTDERR:\n{result.stderr_text}\n"
    97→        )
    98→
    99→        success_code = _handle_successful_attempt(
   100→            result=result,
   101→            output_file=output_file,
   102→            log_file=log_file,
   103→            deps=deps,
   104→            log_sections=log_sections,
   105→        )
   106→        if success_code is not None:
   107→            return success_code
   108→        failure_code = _handle_failed_attempt(
   109→            result=result,
   110→            deps=deps,
   111→            attempt=attempt,
   112→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
   113→            retry_backoff_seconds=config.retry_backoff_seconds,
   114→            log_file=log_file,
   115→            log_sections=log_sections,
   116→        )
   117→        if failure_code is not None:
   118→            return failure_code
   119→
   120→    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
   121→    return 1
   122→

1→"""Packet sanitization, hashing, and artifact layout helpers for review batches."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from copy import deepcopy
     7→from datetime import UTC, datetime
     8→from hashlib import sha256
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PacketValidationError
    13→
    14→_BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS = {
    15→    "narrative",
    16→    "next_command",
    17→    "score_snapshot",
    18→    "strict_target",
    19→    "strict_target_progress",
    20→    "subjective_at_target",
    21→}
    22→
    23→_BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS = {
    24→    "target_strict_score",
    25→    "strict_target_score",
    26→    "target_score",
    27→    "strict_score",
    28→    "objective_score",
    29→    "overall_score",
    30→    "verified_strict_score",
    31→}
    32→
    33→
    34→def write_packet_snapshot(
    35→    packet: dict[str, Any],
    36→    *,
    37→    stamp: str,
    38→    review_packet_dir: Path,
    39→    blind_path: Path,
    40→    safe_write_text_fn,
    41→) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    42→    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
    43→    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    44→    packet_path = review_packet_dir / f"holistic_packet_{stamp}.json"
    45→    safe_write_text_fn(packet_path, json.dumps(packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    46→    blind_packet = _build_blind_packet(packet)
    47→    safe_write_text_fn(blind_path, json.dumps(blind_packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    48→    return packet_path, blind_path
    49→
    50→
    51→def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    52→    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
    53→    blind = deepcopy(packet)
    54→    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
    55→        blind.pop(key, None)
    56→
    57→    config = blind.get("config")
    58→    if isinstance(config, dict):
    59→        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
    60→        if sanitized:
    61→            blind["config"] = sanitized
    62→        else:
    63→            blind.pop("config", None)
    64→    return blind
    65→
    66→
    67→def build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    68→    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
    69→    return _build_blind_packet(packet)
    70→
    71→
    72→def run_stamp() -> str:
    73→    """Stable UTC run stamp for artifact paths."""
    74→    return datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
    75→
    76→
    77→def _sanitize_blind_config(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    78→    """Drop score/target hints from config while preserving unrelated options."""
    79→    sanitized: dict[str, Any] = {}
    80→    for key, value in config.items():
    81→        lowered = key.strip().lower()
    82→        if not lowered:
    83→            continue
    84→        if lowered in _BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS:
    85→            continue
    86→        if "target" in lowered:
    87→            continue
    88→        if lowered.endswith("_score"):
    89→            continue
    90→        sanitized[key] = value
    91→    return sanitized
    92→
    93→
    94→def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    95→    """Compute sha256 hex digest for path contents (or None on read failure)."""
    96→    try:
    97→        data = path.read_bytes()
    98→    except OSError:
    99→        return None
   100→    return sha256(data).hexdigest()
   101→
   102→
   103→def build_batch_import_provenance(
   104→    *,
   105→    runner: str,
   106→    blind_packet_path: Path,
   107→    run_stamp: str,
   108→    batch_indexes: list[int],
   109→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   110→    """Build provenance payload used to trust assessment-bearing imports."""
   111→    packet_hash = sha256_file(blind_packet_path)
   112→    batch_indexes_1 = sorted({int(index) + 1 for index in batch_indexes})
   113→    return {
   114→        "kind": "blind_review_batch_import",
   115→        "blind": True,
   116→        "runner": runner,
   117→        "run_stamp": run_stamp,
   118→        "created_at": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   119→        "batch_count": len(batch_indexes_1),
   120→        "batch_indexes": batch_indexes_1,
   121→        "packet_path": str(blind_packet_path),
   122→        "packet_sha256": packet_hash,
   123→    }
   124→
   125→
   126→def selected_batch_indexes(
   127→    *,
   128→    raw_selection: str | None,
   129→    batch_count: int,
   130→    parse_fn,
   131→    colorize_fn,
   132→) -> list[int]:
   133→    """Validate selected batch indexes or raise CommandError."""
   134→    try:
   135→        selected = parse_fn(raw_selection, batch_count)
   136→    except ValueError as exc:
   137→        raise PacketValidationError(str(exc), exit_code=2) from exc
   138→    if selected:
   139→        return selected
   140→    raise PacketValidationError("no batches selected", exit_code=2)
   141→
   142→
   143→def prepare_run_artifacts(

1→"""Holistic review preparation workflow helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review._context.models import HolisticContext
    11→from desloppify.intelligence.review._prepare.helpers import HOLISTIC_WORKFLOW
    12→from desloppify.intelligence.review._prepare.issue_history import (
    13→    ReviewHistoryOptions,
    14→    build_batch_issue_focus,
    15→    build_issue_history_context,
    16→)
    17→
    18→_NON_PRODUCTION_ZONES = frozenset({"test", "config", "generated", "vendor"})
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→
    22→def collect_allowed_review_files(
    23→    files: list[str],
    24→    lang: object,
    25→    *,
    26→    base_path: Path | None = None,
    27→) -> set[str]:
    28→    """Return relative production-file paths allowed for holistic review batches."""
    29→    allowed: set[str] = set()
    30→    zone_map = getattr(lang, "zone_map", None)
    31→    resolved_base = base_path.resolve() if isinstance(base_path, Path) else None
    32→    for filepath in files:
    33→        if not isinstance(filepath, str):
    34→            continue
    35→        normalized = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    36→        if not normalized:
    37→            continue
    38→        if zone_map is not None:
    39→            zone_get = getattr(zone_map, "get", None)
    40→            zone = zone_get(filepath) if callable(zone_get) else None
    41→            zone_value = getattr(zone, "value", zone)
    42→            if zone_value is None:
    43→                zone_value = "production"
    44→            if not isinstance(zone_value, str):
    45→                zone_value = str(zone_value)
    46→            if zone_value in _NON_PRODUCTION_ZONES:
    47→                continue
    48→        allowed.add(normalized)
    49→        allowed.add(rel(filepath))
    50→        if resolved_base is not None:
    51→            try:
    52→                resolved_path = Path(filepath).resolve()
    53→            except OSError as exc:
    54→                logger.debug("Skipping invalid review file path %s: %s", filepath, exc)
    55→                continue
    56→            if resolved_path.is_relative_to(resolved_base):
    57→                allowed.add(resolved_path.relative_to(resolved_base).as_posix())
    58→    return allowed
    59→
    60→
    61→def file_in_allowed_scope(filepath: object, allowed_files: set[str]) -> bool:
    62→    """True when *filepath* resolves to a currently in-scope review file."""
    63→    if not isinstance(filepath, str):
    64→        return False
    65→    normalized = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    66→    if not normalized:
    67→        return False
    68→    if normalized in allowed_files:
    69→        return True
    70→    return rel(filepath) in allowed_files
    71→
    72→
    73→def filter_issue_focus_to_scope(
    74→    issue_focus: object,
    75→    allowed_files: set[str],
    76→) -> dict[str, object] | None:
    77→    """Drop out-of-scope related_files from historical issue focus payload."""
    78→    if not isinstance(issue_focus, dict):
    79→        return None
    80→    issues_raw = issue_focus.get("issues", [])
    81→    issues: list[dict[str, object]] = []
    82→    if isinstance(issues_raw, list):
    83→        for raw_issue in issues_raw:
    84→            if not isinstance(raw_issue, dict):
    85→                continue
    86→            issue = dict(raw_issue)
    87→            related_raw = issue.get("related_files", [])
    88→            if isinstance(related_raw, list):
    89→                issue["related_files"] = [
    90→                    path for path in related_raw if file_in_allowed_scope(path, allowed_files)
    91→                ]
    92→            issues.append(issue)
    93→    scoped = dict(issue_focus)
    94→    scoped["issues"] = issues
    95→    scoped["selected_count"] = len(issues)
    96→    return scoped
    97→
    98→
    99→def filter_batches_to_file_scope(
   100→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   101→    *,
   102→    allowed_files: set[str],
   103→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   104→    """Strip out-of-scope files/signals from review batches."""
   105→    if not allowed_files:
   106→        return []
   107→
   108→    scoped_batches: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   109→    for raw_batch in batches:
   110→        if not isinstance(raw_batch, dict):
   111→            continue
   112→        batch = dict(raw_batch)
   113→        files_to_read = batch.get("files_to_read", [])
   114→        if isinstance(files_to_read, list):
   115→            scoped_files = [
   116→                filepath
   117→                for filepath in files_to_read
   118→                if file_in_allowed_scope(filepath, allowed_files)
   119→            ]
   120→        else:
   121→            scoped_files = []
   122→        batch["files_to_read"] = scoped_files
   123→
   124→        concern_signals = batch.get("concern_signals", [])
   125→        if isinstance(concern_signals, list):
   126→            batch["concern_signals"] = [
   127→                signal
   128→                for signal in concern_signals
   129→                if isinstance(signal, dict)
   130→                and file_in_allowed_scope(signal.get("file", ""), allowed_files)
   131→            ]
   132→            if "concern_signal_count" in batch:
   133→                batch["concern_signal_count"] = len(batch["concern_signals"])
   134→
   135→        issue_focus = filter_issue_focus_to_scope(
   136→            batch.get("historical_issue_focus"),
   137→            allowed_files,
   138→        )
   139→        if issue_focus is not None:
   140→            batch["historical_issue_focus"] = issue_focus
   141→
   142→        has_seed_files = bool(batch["files_to_read"])
   143→        has_signals = bool(batch.get("concern_signals"))
   144→        if has_seed_files or has_signals:
   145→            scoped_batches.append(batch)
   146→    return scoped_batches
   147→

   149→def _resolve_review_files(
   150→    path: Path,
   151→    lang: object,
   152→    options: object,
   153→) -> tuple[list[str], set[str]]:
   154→    all_files = (
   155→        options.files
   156→        if options.files is not None
   157→        else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
   158→    )
   159→    return all_files, collect_allowed_review_files(all_files, lang, base_path=path)
   160→
   161→
   162→def _build_review_contexts(
   163→    *,
   164→    path: Path,
   165→    lang: object,
   166→    state: dict,
   167→    all_files: list[str],
   168→    is_file_cache_enabled_fn,
   169→    enable_file_cache_fn,
   170→    disable_file_cache_fn,
   171→    build_holistic_context_fn,
   172→    build_review_context_fn,
   173→) -> tuple[HolisticContext, object]:
   174→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled_fn()
   175→    if not already_cached:
   176→        enable_file_cache_fn()
   177→    try:
   178→        context = HolisticContext.from_raw(
   179→            build_holistic_context_fn(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   180→        )
   181→        review_ctx = build_review_context_fn(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   182→    finally:
   183→        if not already_cached:
   184→            disable_file_cache_fn()
   185→    return context, review_ctx
   186→

def write_packet_snapshot(
    packet: dict[str, Any],
    *,
    stamp: str,
    review_packet_dir: Path,
    blind_path: Path,
    safe_write_text_fn,
) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
--
    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
        blind.pop(key, None)

    config = blind.get("config")
    if isinstance(config, dict):
        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
        if sanitized:
            blind["config"] = sanitized
        else:
            blind.pop("config", None)
    return blind


def build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
    return _build_blind_packet(packet)


def run_stamp() -> str:
    """Stable UTC run stamp for artifact paths."""
    return datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
--
        if lowered in _BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS:
            continue
        if "target" in lowered:
            continue
        if lowered.endswith("_score"):
            continue
        sanitized[key] = value
    return sanitized


def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    """Compute sha256 hex digest for path contents (or None on read failure)."""
    try:
        data = path.read_bytes()
    except OSError:
        return None
    return sha256(data).hexdigest()

## Research Findings: Two Review Paths - Prompt Delivery Analysis

I've traced through both the batch and external review paths. Here's exactly how the prompts are delivered in each case:

---

## BATCH PATH: orchestrator.py → execution.py → subagent

**File paths involved:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/orchestrator.py` (lines 95-178)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/execution.py` (lines 658-933)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py` (lines 388-412)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_packets.py` (lines 34-100)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_process.py` (lines 24-44)

### How it works:

1. **Packet preparation** (orchestrator.py, lines 95-178, `_load_or_prepare_packet`):
   - Creates a FULL packet with:
     - `system_prompt` (string)
     - `dimension_prompts` (dict of dimension → prompt definitions)
     - `holistic_context` (scan evidence, structural data, etc.)
     - Metadata like `narrative`, `next_command`, scores, etc.

2. **Two files written to disk**:
   - **Immutable packet**: `holistic_packet_<stamp>.json` (full packet)
   - **Blind packet**: `<blind_path>` (same as immutable, but with these keys stripped):
     - Drops: `narrative`, `next_command`, `score_snapshot`, `strict_target`, `strict_target_progress`, `subjective_at_target`
     - Also strips config keys matching score patterns: `target_strict_score`, `strict_target_score`, `target_score`, `strict_score`, `objective_score`, `overall_score`, `verified_strict_score`, or containing "target" or ending with "_score"

3. **Batch prompts generated** (runner_packets.py, lines 143-186, `prepare_run_artifacts`):
   - For each selected batch, calls `build_prompt_fn` (which is `batch_core_mod.build_batch_prompt`)
   - Prompt is generated via `render_batch_prompt()` (prompt_template.py, lines 388-412)
   - The prompt includes:
     - Metadata block with `Blind packet: {packet_path}` (reference to the FILE path, not content)
     - Scoring frame instructions
     - Scan evidence note
     - Seed files
     - Historical focus (from batch)
     - Mechanical concern signals
     - Task requirements (instruction 1: "Read the blind packet and follow `system_prompt` constraints exactly")
     - Scope enums
     - Output schema
   - **Saved to**: `prompts/batch-{idx+1}.md`

4. **Subagent execution** (runner_process.py, lines 24-44):
   - Command: `codex exec -C {repo_root} ... {prompt_text}`
   - The **prompt text itself** is passed as the command-line argument to codex
   - The prompt contains:
     - All the batch-specific instructions
     - A FILE PATH reference to the blind packet: `Blind packet: {packet_path}`
   - **The subagent MUST**:
     - Read the blind packet file from disk (at the path specified in the prompt)
     - Extract `system_prompt` and `dimension_prompts` from that file
     - Use those to score the batch's dimensions

### Key insight for BATCH path:
- **The subagent gets TWO inputs**:
  1. The batch prompt (passed as command-line argument or read from file)
  2. The blind packet file path (referenced IN the prompt)
- **The subagent must independently read the blind packet from disk** to access `system_prompt` and `dimension_prompts`
- Both immutable and blind packets are identical except for score-anchoring metadata removal
- **The system_prompt and dimension_prompts are NOT embedded in the batch prompt itself** — they must be read from the blind packet file

---

## EXTERNAL PATH: external.py → claude_launch_prompt.md

**File paths involved:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py` (lines 188-217)

### How it works:

1. **Session setup** (external.py, lines 220-338, `do_external_start`):
   - Creates same full packet as batch path
   - Writes same immutable + blind packets
   - Creates a **template JSON** file with:
     - `session.id` and `session.token` (for verification on submit)
     - Scaffold of assessment structure
   - Generates launch prompt via `_build_claude_launch_prompt()` (lines 188-217)

2. **Launch prompt content** (lines 188-217):
   - Returns a markdown string with:
     ```
     # Claude Blind Reviewer Launch Prompt

     You are an isolated blind reviewer...

     Blind packet: {blind_path}
     Template JSON: {template_path}
     Output JSON path: {output_path}

     Requirements:
     1. Read ONLY the blind packet and repository code.
     2. Start from the template JSON...
     3. Keep `session.id` exactly `{session_id}`...
     4. Keep `session.token` exactly `{token}`...
     5. Output must be valid JSON with top-level keys: session, assessments, issues.
     6. Every issue must include: dimension, identifier, summary, related_files, evidence, suggestion, confidence.
     7. Do not include provenance metadata (CLI injects canonical provenance).
     8. Return JSON only (no markdown fences).
     ```
   - **Saved to**: `claude_launch_prompt.md` (human-readable copy/paste prompt)

3. **What the external reviewer gets**:
   - A markdown file with instructions (the launch prompt)
   - File paths to:
     - Blind packet (same structure as batch path)
     - Template JSON (session structure only)
     - Expected output path (where to write JSON result)
   - **No embedded system_prompt or dimension_prompts**
   - **No batch-specific instruction details** (unlike batch path)

4. **Subagent execution** (external reviewer, manual):
   - User/external reviewer copies the launch prompt into a **context-isolated Claude conversation**
   - Claude reads the blind packet file
   - Claude reads the template JSON file
   - Claude examines the codebase
   - Claude writes output JSON to the specified path
   - User submits via `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`

### Key insight for EXTERNAL path:
- **The external reviewer gets minimal instructions** — just the launch prompt (references to files, not content)
- **The external reviewer must independently read**:
  - The blind packet file (to get `system_prompt` and `dimension_prompts`)
  - The template JSON file (to preserve session metadata)
- **No batch-specific batching logic** — the entire codebase is in scope for a single review session
- **On submit**: CLI reads the output JSON, validates it, wraps it with canonical provenance, and imports it

---

## COMPARISON TABLE

| Aspect | BATCH Path | EXTERNAL Path |
|--------|-----------|---------------|
| **Prompt delivery** | Text passed to `codex exec` command | Markdown file for copy/paste |
| **System/Dim prompts** | Referenced in blind packet file path | Referenced in blind packet file path |
| **Blind packet location** | Disk (path in prompt) | Disk (path in launch prompt) |
| **Batch specifics** | Full batch instructions embedded in prompt | No batch-specific instructions |
| **Scope** | Single batch (seed files + exploration) | Full codebase |
| **Session tracking** | Via run summary + batch status | Via session.json with expiry |
| **Subagent context** | Codex runner, ephemeral | External Claude conversation |

---

## DO BOTH PATHS DELIVER THE SAME INFORMATION?

**Not exactly, but structurally yes:**

- **SAME**: Both deliver `system_prompt` and `dimension_prompts` via the blind packet file (not embedded in task prompt)
- **SAME**: Both scrub score-anchoring metadata from the blind packet
- **SAME**: Both use the same evaluation dimensions and scoring rubric
- **DIFFERENT**: Batch path includes batch-specific seed files, historical issues, and mechanical concern signals in the **task prompt itself**. External path has none of this — the reviewer must discover issues through code exploration.
- **DIFFERENT**: Batch path batches by dimension/risk (focused investigation). External path reviews everything in one session.

**The architecture is consistent**: In both cases, the reviewer's **task prompt is separate from the dimension/system prompts**, and those are always retrieved from the blind packet file at runtime. The difference is in **what else is in the task prompt** to guide the reviewer's investigation.

   286→def _validate_run_dir(run_dir: Path) -> tuple[dict, Path, str]:
   287→    """Validate run directory, load summary, and return (summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path).
   288→
   289→    Raises CommandError on any validation failure.
   290→    """
   291→    if not run_dir.is_dir():
   292→        raise CommandError(f"run directory not found: {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   293→
   294→    summary_path = run_dir / "run_summary.json"
   295→    if not summary_path.exists():
   296→        raise CommandError(f"no run_summary.json in {run_dir}", exit_code=1)
   297→    try:
   298→        summary = json.loads(summary_path.read_text())
   299→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   300→        raise CommandError(f"Error reading run summary: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   301→
   302→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])
   303→    blind_packet_path = Path(str(summary.get("blind_packet", "")))
   304→    immutable_packet_path = str(summary.get("immutable_packet", ""))
   305→
   306→    if not successful:
   307→        raise CommandError("no successful batches in run summary.", exit_code=1)
   308→    if not blind_packet_path.exists():
   309→        raise PacketValidationError(f"blind packet not found: {blind_packet_path}", exit_code=1)
   310→
   311→    try:
   312→        packet = json.loads(Path(immutable_packet_path).read_text())
   313→    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   314→        raise PacketValidationError(f"Error reading immutable packet: {exc}", exit_code=1) from exc
   315→
   316→    summary["_packet"] = packet
   317→    return summary, blind_packet_path, immutable_packet_path

   320→def do_import_run(
   321→    run_dir_path: str,
   322→    state: dict,
   323→    lang,

   325→    *,
   326→    config: dict | None = None,
   327→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   328→    scan_after_import: bool = False,
   329→    scan_path: str = ".",
   330→) -> None:
   331→    """Re-import results from a completed run directory.
   332→
   333→    Replays the merge+provenance+import step that normally runs at the end of
   334→    ``--run-batches``.  Useful when the original pipeline was interrupted (e.g.
   335→    broken pipe from background execution) but all batch results completed.
   336→    """
   337→    run_dir = Path(run_dir_path)
   338→    summary, blind_packet_path, _immutable_path = _validate_run_dir(run_dir)
   339→
   340→    runner = str(summary.get("runner", "codex"))
   341→    stamp = str(summary.get("run_stamp", ""))
   342→    successful = summary.get("successful_batches", [])
   343→    packet = summary.pop("_packet", {})
   344→    allowed_dims = {str(d) for d in packet.get("dimensions", []) if isinstance(d, str)}
   345→
   346→    # -- locate and parse raw batch results --
   347→    results_dir = run_dir / "results"
   348→    selected_indexes = [idx - 1 for idx in successful]  # convert 1-based to 0-based
   349→    output_files = {
   350→        idx: results_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.raw.txt"
   351→        for idx in selected_indexes
   352→    }
   353→
   354→    missing = [idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes if not output_files[idx].exists()]
   355→    if missing:
   356→        raise CommandError(f"missing result files for batches: {missing}", exit_code=1)
   357→
   358→    batch_results, failures = collect_batch_results(
   359→        selected_indexes=selected_indexes,
   360→        failures=[],
   361→        output_files=output_files,
   362→        allowed_dims=allowed_dims,
   363→        extract_payload_fn=lambda raw: batch_core_mod.extract_json_payload(raw, log_fn=log),
   364→        normalize_result_fn=lambda payload, dims: batch_core_mod.normalize_batch_result(
   365→            payload,
   366→            dims,
   367→            max_batch_issues=max_batch_issues_for_dimension_count(len(dims)),
   368→            abstraction_sub_axes=ABSTRACTION_SUB_AXES,
   369→        ),
   370→    )
   371→
   372→    if not batch_results:
   373→        raise CommandError("no valid batch results could be parsed.", exit_code=1)
   374→
   375→    print(colorize(f"  Parsed {len(batch_results)} batch results from {run_dir}", "bold"))
   376→    if failures:
   377→        print(colorize(f"  Warning: {len(failures)} batches failed to parse: {[f + 1 for f in failures]}", "yellow"))
   378→
   379→    # -- merge --
   380→    merged = _merge_batch_results(batch_results)
   381→
   382→    # -- build provenance --
   383→    successful_indexes = [idx for idx in selected_indexes if idx not in set(failures)]
   384→    merged["provenance"] = build_batch_import_provenance(
   385→        runner=runner,
   386→        blind_packet_path=blind_packet_path,
   387→        run_stamp=stamp,
   388→        batch_indexes=successful_indexes,
   389→    )
   390→
   391→    # -- write merged output --
   392→    merged_path = run_dir / "holistic_issues_merged.json"
   393→    safe_write_text(merged_path, json.dumps(merged, indent=2) + "\n")
   394→    print(colorize(f"  Merged output: {merged_path}", "bold"))
   395→
   396→    # -- import with trusted source --
   397→    _do_import(
   398→        str(merged_path),
   399→        state,
   400→        lang,
   401→        state_file,
   402→        config=config,
   403→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   404→        trusted_assessment_source=True,
   405→        trusted_assessment_label=f"trusted import-run replay from {run_dir.name}",
   406→    )
   407→
   408→    # -- optional follow-up scan --
   409→    if scan_after_import:
   410→        lang_name = getattr(lang, "name", None) or str(getattr(lang, "lang", ""))
   411→        if lang_name:
   412→            run_followup_scan(
   413→                lang_name=lang_name,
   414→                scan_path=scan_path,
   415→                deps=FollowupScanDeps(
   416→                    project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
   417→                    timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   418→                    python_executable=sys.executable,
   419→                    subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   420→                    timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   421→                    colorize_fn=colorize,
   422→                ),
   423→            )
   424→
```

    86→
    87→def _runtime_expectation(
    88→    *,
    89→    total_batches: int,
    90→    run_parallel: bool,
    91→    max_parallel_batches: int,
    92→    batch_timeout_seconds: int,
    93→) -> tuple[int, int]:
    94→    effective_workers = min(total_batches, max_parallel_batches) if run_parallel else 1
    95→    waves = max(1, math.ceil(total_batches / max(1, effective_workers)))
    96→    worst_case_minutes = max(1, math.ceil((waves * batch_timeout_seconds) / 60))
    97→    return effective_workers, worst_case_minutes
    98→

   175→def _build_batch_tracking(
   176→    *,
   177→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   178→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
   179→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   180→    log_files: dict[int, Path],
   181→) -> tuple[str, dict[int, int], set[int], dict[str, dict[str, object]]]:
   182→    summary_created_at = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   183→    batch_positions = {batch_idx: pos + 1 for pos, batch_idx in enumerate(selected_indexes)}
   184→    stall_warned_batches: set[int] = set()
   185→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {
   186→        str(idx + 1): {
   187→            "position": batch_positions.get(idx, 0),
   188→            "status": "pending",
   189→            "prompt_path": str(prompt_files[idx]),
   190→            "result_path": str(output_files[idx]),
   191→            "log_path": str(log_files[idx]),
   192→        }
   193→        for idx in selected_indexes
   194→    }
   195→    return summary_created_at, batch_positions, stall_warned_batches, batch_status
   196→
   197→
   198→def _handle_heartbeat_progress(
   199→    *,
   200→    details: dict[str, object],
   201→    total_batches: int,
   202→    stall_warning_seconds: int,
   203→    stall_warned_batches: set[int],
   204→    append_run_log,
   205→    colorize_fn,
   206→) -> None:
   207→    active = details.get("active_batches")
   208→    queued = details.get("queued_batches", [])
   209→    elapsed = details.get("elapsed_seconds", {})
   210→    if not isinstance(active, list):
   211→        active = []
   212→    if not isinstance(queued, list):
   213→        queued = []
   214→    if not active and not queued:
   215→        return
   216→
   217→    segments: list[str] = []
   218→    for idx in active[:6]:
   219→        secs = 0
   220→        if isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   221→            raw_secs = elapsed.get(idx, 0)
   222→            secs = int(raw_secs) if isinstance(raw_secs, int | float) else 0
   223→        segments.append(f"#{idx + 1}:{secs}s")
   224→    if len(active) > 6:
   225→        segments.append(f"+{len(active) - 6} more")
   226→
   227→    queued_segment = f", queued {len(queued)}" if queued else ""
   228→    print(
   229→        colorize_fn(
   230→            "  Batch heartbeat: "
   231→            f"{len(active)}/{total_batches} active{queued_segment} "
   232→            f"({', '.join(segments) if segments else 'running batches pending'})",
   233→            "dim",
   234→        )
   235→    )
   236→    elapsed_text = ""
   237→    if isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   238→        elapsed_text = ", ".join(f"{idx + 1}:{elapsed.get(idx, 0)}" for idx in active)
   239→    append_run_log(
   240→        "heartbeat "
   241→        f"active={[idx + 1 for idx in active]} queued={[idx + 1 for idx in queued]} "
   242→        f"elapsed={{{elapsed_text}}}"
   243→    )
   244→
   245→    if stall_warning_seconds <= 0 or not isinstance(elapsed, dict):
   246→        return
   247→    slow_active = [
   248→        idx
   249→        for idx in active
   250→        if isinstance(elapsed.get(idx), int | float)
   251→        and int(elapsed.get(idx) or 0) >= stall_warning_seconds
   252→    ]
   253→    newly_warned = [idx for idx in slow_active if idx not in stall_warned_batches]
   254→    if not newly_warned:
   255→        return
   256→
   257→    stall_warned_batches.update(newly_warned)
   258→    warning_message = (
   259→        "  Stall warning: batches "
   260→        f"{[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]} exceeded "
   261→        f"{stall_warning_seconds}s elapsed. "
   262→        "This may be normal for long runs; review run.log and batch logs."
   263→    )
   264→    print(colorize_fn(warning_message, "yellow"))
   265→    append_run_log(
   266→        "stall-warning "
   267→        f"threshold={stall_warning_seconds}s batches={[idx + 1 for idx in sorted(newly_warned)]}"
   268→    )
   269→
   270→
   271→def _handle_batch_progress(
   272→    *,
   273→    progress_event: BatchProgressEvent,
   274→    batch_positions: dict[int, int],
   275→    total_batches: int,
   276→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
   277→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   278→    log_files: dict[int, Path],
   279→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   280→    stall_warned_batches: set[int],
   281→    append_run_log,
   282→    colorize_fn,
   283→) -> None:
   284→    batch_index = progress_event.batch_index
   285→    event = progress_event.event
   286→    code = progress_event.code
   287→    details = progress_event.details
   288→
   289→    position = batch_positions.get(batch_index, 0)
   290→    key = str(batch_index + 1)
   291→    state = batch_status.setdefault(
   292→        key,
   293→        {
   294→            "position": position,
   295→            "status": "pending",
   296→            "prompt_path": str(prompt_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   297→            "result_path": str(output_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   298→            "log_path": str(log_files.get(batch_index, "")),
   299→        },
   300→    )
   301→
   302→    if event == "queued":
   303→        state["status"] = "queued"
   304→        print(colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{total_batches} queued (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
   305→        append_run_log(f"batch-queued batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{total_batches}")
   306→        return
   307→
   308→    if event == "start":
   309→        state["status"] = "running"
   310→        state["started_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   311→        print(colorize_fn(f"  Batch {position}/{total_batches} started (#{batch_index + 1})", "dim"))
   312→        append_run_log(f"batch-start batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{total_batches}")
   313→        return
   314→
   315→    if event != "done":
   316→        return
   317→    status = "done" if code == 0 else f"failed ({code})"
   318→    tone = "dim" if code == 0 else "yellow"
   319→    elapsed_seconds = details.get("elapsed_seconds")
   320→    elapsed_suffix = ""
   321→    if isinstance(elapsed_seconds, int | float):
   322→        elapsed_suffix = f" in {int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))}s"
   323→        state["elapsed_seconds"] = int(max(0, elapsed_seconds))
   324→    state["status"] = "succeeded" if code == 0 else "failed"
   325→    state["exit_code"] = int(code) if isinstance(code, int) else code
   326→    state["completed_at"] = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   327→    stall_warned_batches.discard(batch_index)
   328→    print(
   329→        colorize_fn(
   330→            f"  Batch {position}/{total_batches} {status}{elapsed_suffix} (#{batch_index + 1})",
   331→            tone,
   332→        )
   333→    )
   334→    append_run_log(
   335→        f"batch-done batch={batch_index + 1} position={position}/{total_batches} "
   336→        f"code={code} elapsed={state.get('elapsed_seconds', 0)}"
   337→    )
   338→
   339→
   340→def _build_progress_reporter(
   341→    *,
   342→    batch_positions: dict[int, int],
   343→    total_batches: int,
   344→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path],
   345→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
   346→    log_files: dict[int, Path],
   347→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   348→    stall_warned_batches: set[int],
   349→    stall_warning_seconds: int,
   350→    append_run_log,
   351→    colorize_fn,
   352→) -> Callable[[BatchProgressEvent], None]:
   353→    def _report_progress(progress_event: BatchProgressEvent) -> None:
   354→        if progress_event.event == "heartbeat":
   355→            _handle_heartbeat_progress(
   356→                details=progress_event.details,
   357→                total_batches=total_batches,
   358→                stall_warning_seconds=stall_warning_seconds,
   359→                stall_warned_batches=stall_warned_batches,
   360→                append_run_log=append_run_log,
   361→                colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
   362→            )
   363→            return
   364→        _handle_batch_progress(
   365→            progress_event=progress_event,
   366→            batch_positions=batch_positions,
   367→            total_batches=total_batches,
   368→            prompt_files=prompt_files,
   369→            output_files=output_files,
   370→            log_files=log_files,
   371→            batch_status=batch_status,
   372→            stall_warned_batches=stall_warned_batches,
   373→            append_run_log=append_run_log,
   374→            colorize_fn=colorize_fn,
   375→        )
   376→
   377→    return _report_progress
   378→
   379→
   380→def _build_summary_writer(
   381→    *,
   382→    run_summary_path: Path,

   384→    batch_status: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   385→    safe_write_text_fn,
