```
    36→
    37→
    38→def parse_holistic_import_payload(
    39→    data: ReviewImportPayload | dict[str, Any],
    40→) -> tuple[list[ReviewIssuePayload], dict[str, Any] | None, list[str]]:
    41→    """Parse strict holistic import payload object."""
    42→    payload = parse_review_import_payload(data, mode_name="Holistic")
    43→    return payload.issues, payload.assessments, payload.reviewed_files
    44→
    45→
    46→def import_holistic_issues(
    47→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
    48→    state: StateModel,
    49→    lang_name: str,
    50→    *,
    51→    project_root: Path | str | None = None,
    52→    utc_now_fn=utc_now,
    53→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    54→    """Import holistic (codebase-wide) issues into state."""
    55→    payload: ReviewImportEnvelope = parse_review_import_payload(
    56→        issues_data,
    57→        mode_name="Holistic",
    58→    )
    59→    issues_list = payload.issues
    60→    assessments = payload.assessments
    61→    reviewed_files = payload.reviewed_files
    62→    review_scope = issues_data.get("review_scope", {})
    63→    if not isinstance(review_scope, dict):
    64→        review_scope = {}
    65→    review_scope.setdefault("full_sweep_included", None)
    66→    scope_full_sweep = review_scope.get("full_sweep_included")
    67→    if not isinstance(scope_full_sweep, bool):
    68→        scope_full_sweep = None
    69→
    70→    if assessments:
    71→        store_assessments(
    72→            state,
    73→            assessments,
    74→            source="holistic",
    75→            utc_now_fn=utc_now_fn,
    76→        )
    77→
    78→    _, holistic_prompts, _ = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang_name)
    79→    valid_dimensions = {
    80→        normalize_dimension_name(dim)
    81→        for dim in holistic_prompts
    82→        if isinstance(dim, str)
    83→    }
    84→    review_issues, skipped, dismissed_concerns = _validate_and_build_issues(
    85→        issues_list,
    86→        holistic_prompts,
    87→        lang_name,
    88→    )
    89→    imported_dimensions = _collect_imported_dimensions(
    90→        issues_list=issues_list,
    91→        review_issues=review_issues,
    92→        assessments=assessments if isinstance(assessments, dict) else None,
    93→        review_scope=review_scope,
    94→        valid_dimensions=valid_dimensions,
    95→    )
    96→
    97→    if dismissed_concerns:
    98→        from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
    99→
   100→        store = state.setdefault("concern_dismissals", {})
   101→        now = utc_now_fn()
   102→        current_concerns = generate_concerns(state)
   103→        concern_sources = {
   104→            concern.fingerprint: list(concern.source_issues)
   105→            for concern in current_concerns
   106→        }
   107→        for dismissal in dismissed_concerns:
   108→            fingerprint = dismissal["fingerprint"]
   109→            store[fingerprint] = {
   110→                "dismissed_at": now,
   111→                "reasoning": dismissal.get("reasoning", ""),
   112→                "concern_type": dismissal.get("concern_type", ""),
   113→                "concern_file": dismissal.get("concern_file", ""),
   114→                "source_issue_ids": concern_sources.get(fingerprint, []),
   115→            }
   116→
   117→    potentials = _lang_potentials(state, lang_name)
   118→    existing_review = potentials.get("review", 0)
   119→    potentials["review"] = max(existing_review, HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL)
   120→
   121→    concern_count = sum(1 for issue in review_issues if issue.get("detector") == "concerns")
   122→    if concern_count:
   123→        potentials["concerns"] = max(potentials.get("concerns", 0), concern_count)
   124→
   125→    merge_potentials_dict: dict[str, int] = {"review": potentials.get("review", 0)}
   126→    if potentials.get("concerns", 0) > 0:
   127→        merge_potentials_dict["concerns"] = potentials["concerns"]
   128→
   129→    diff = merge_scan(
   130→        state,
   131→        review_issues,
   132→        options=MergeScanOptions(
   133→            lang=lang_name,
   134→            potentials=merge_potentials_dict,
   135→            merge_potentials=True,
   136→        ),
   137→    )
   138→
   139→    new_ids = {issue["id"] for issue in review_issues}
   140→    _auto_resolve_stale_holistic(
   141→        state,
   142→        new_ids,
   143→        diff,
   144→        utc_now_fn,
   145→        imported_dimensions=imported_dimensions,
   146→        full_sweep_included=scope_full_sweep,
   147→    )
   148→
   149→    if skipped:
   150→        diff["skipped"] = len(skipped)
   151→        diff["skipped_details"] = skipped
   152→
   153→    update_reviewed_file_cache(
   154→        state,
   155→        reviewed_files,
   156→        project_root=project_root,
   157→        utc_now_fn=utc_now_fn,
   158→    )
   159→    resolve_reviewed_file_coverage_issues(
   160→        state,
   161→        diff,
   162→        reviewed_files,
   163→        utc_now_fn=utc_now_fn,
   164→    )
   165→    update_holistic_review_cache(
   166→        state,
   167→        issues_list,
   168→        lang_name=lang_name,
   169→        review_scope=review_scope,
   170→        utc_now_fn=utc_now_fn,
   171→    )
   172→    resolve_holistic_coverage_issues(state, diff, utc_now_fn=utc_now_fn)
   173→
   174→    from desloppify.engine.concerns import cleanup_stale_dismissals
   175→
   176→    cleanup_stale_dismissals(state)
   177→
   178→    return diff
   179→
   180→
   181→__all__ = [
   182→    "import_holistic_issues",
   183→    "parse_holistic_import_payload",
   184→    "resolve_holistic_coverage_issues",
   185→    "resolve_reviewed_file_coverage_issues",
   186→    "update_holistic_review_cache",
```

```
1→"""Serial/parallel execution loops for review batch tasks."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import threading
     7→from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed
     8→from concurrent.futures import TimeoutError as FuturesTimeoutError
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.output.fallbacks import log_best_effort_failure
    11→
    12→from ._runner_parallel_progress import (
    13→    _RUNNER_CALLBACK_EXCEPTIONS,
    14→    _RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS,
    15→    _emit_progress,
    16→    _record_execution_error,
    17→    _record_progress_error,
    18→)
    19→from ._runner_parallel_types import BatchTask
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→def _execute_serial(
    25→    *,
    26→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
    27→    indexes: list[int],
    28→    progress_fn,
    29→    error_log_fn,
    30→    clock_fn,
    31→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
    32→) -> list[int]:
    33→    """Run tasks one at a time — no threads, no closures."""
    34→    failures: set[int] = set()
    35→    for idx in indexes:
    36→        t0 = float(clock_fn())
    37→        start_error = _emit_progress(
    38→            progress_fn,
    39→            idx,
    40→            "start",
    41→            None,
    42→            details={"max_workers": 1},
    43→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
    44→        )
    45→        if start_error is not None:
    46→            _record_execution_error(
    47→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    48→                failures=failures,
    49→                idx=idx,
    50→                exc=start_error,
    51→            )
    52→        try:
    53→            code = tasks[idx]()
    54→        except _RUNNER_TASK_EXCEPTIONS as exc:
    55→            _record_execution_error(
    56→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    57→                failures=failures,
    58→                idx=idx,
    59→                exc=exc,
    60→            )
    61→            code = 1
    62→        if code != 0:
    63→            failures.add(idx)
    64→        done_error = _emit_progress(
    65→            progress_fn,
    66→            idx,
    67→            "done",
    68→            code,
    69→            details={"elapsed_seconds": int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - t0))},
    70→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
    71→        )
    72→        if done_error is not None:
    73→            _record_execution_error(
    74→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
    75→                failures=failures,
    76→                idx=idx,
    77→                exc=done_error,
    78→            )
    79→    return sorted(failures)
    80→
    81→
    82→def _resolve_parallel_runtime(
    83→    *,
    84→    indexes: list[int],
    85→    max_parallel_workers,
    86→    heartbeat_seconds,
    87→) -> tuple[int, float | None]:
    88→    requested = (
    89→        int(max_parallel_workers)
    90→        if isinstance(max_parallel_workers, int) and max_parallel_workers > 0
    91→        else 8
    92→    )
    93→    max_workers = max(1, min(len(indexes), requested))
    94→    heartbeat = (
    95→        float(heartbeat_seconds)
    96→        if isinstance(heartbeat_seconds, int | float) and heartbeat_seconds > 0
    97→        else None
    98→    )
    99→    return max_workers, heartbeat
   100→
   101→
   102→def _run_parallel_task(
   103→    *,
   104→    idx: int,
   105→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
   106→    progress_fn,
   107→    error_log_fn,
   108→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   109→    max_workers: int,
   110→    progress_failures: set[int],
   111→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   112→    lock: threading.Lock,
   113→    clock_fn,
   114→) -> int:
   115→    with lock:
   116→        started_at[idx] = float(clock_fn())
   117→    progress_error = _emit_progress(
   118→        progress_fn,
   119→        idx,
   120→        "start",
   121→        None,
   122→        details={"max_workers": max_workers},
   123→        contract_cache=contract_cache,
   124→    )
   125→    if progress_error is not None:
   126→        _record_progress_error(
   127→            idx=idx,
   128→            err=progress_error,
   129→            progress_failures=progress_failures,
   130→            lock=lock,
   131→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   132→        )
   133→    return tasks[idx]()
   134→
   135→
   136→def _queue_parallel_tasks(
   137→    *,
   138→    executor: ThreadPoolExecutor,
   139→    indexes: list[int],
   140→    tasks: dict[int, BatchTask],
   141→    progress_fn,
   142→    error_log_fn,
   143→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   144→    max_workers: int,
   145→    failures: set[int],
   146→    progress_failures: set[int],
   147→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   148→    lock: threading.Lock,
   149→    clock_fn,
   150→) -> dict:
   151→    futures: dict = {}
   152→    for idx in indexes:
   153→        queue_error = _emit_progress(
   154→            progress_fn,
   155→            idx,
   156→            "queued",
   157→            None,
   158→            details={"max_workers": max_workers},
   159→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
   160→        )
   161→        if queue_error is not None:
   162→            _record_progress_error(
   163→                idx=idx,
   164→                err=queue_error,
   165→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   166→                lock=lock,
   167→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   168→            )
   169→            failures.add(idx)
   170→        futures[
   171→            executor.submit(
   172→                _run_parallel_task,
   173→                idx=idx,
   174→                tasks=tasks,
   175→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
   176→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   177→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   178→                max_workers=max_workers,
   179→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   180→                started_at=started_at,
   181→                lock=lock,
   182→                clock_fn=clock_fn,
   183→            )
   184→        ] = idx
   185→    return futures
   186→
   187→
   188→def _complete_parallel_future(
   189→    *,
   190→    future,
   191→    futures: dict,
   192→    progress_fn,
   193→    error_log_fn,
   194→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   195→    failures: set[int],
   196→    progress_failures: set[int],
   197→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   198→    lock: threading.Lock,
   199→    clock_fn,
   200→) -> None:
```

```
   255→def _drain_parallel_completions(
   256→    *,
   257→    pending: set,
   258→    futures: dict,
   259→    heartbeat: float | None,
   260→    indexes: list[int],
   261→    progress_fn,
   262→    error_log_fn,
   263→    contract_cache: dict[int, str],
   264→    failures: set[int],
   265→    progress_failures: set[int],
   266→    started_at: dict[int, float],
   267→    lock: threading.Lock,
   268→    clock_fn,
   269→) -> None:
   270→    if heartbeat is None:
   271→        for future in as_completed(pending):
   272→            _complete_parallel_future(
   273→                future=future,
   274→                futures=futures,
   275→                progress_fn=progress_fn,
   276→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   277→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   278→                failures=failures,
   279→                progress_failures=progress_failures,
   280→                started_at=started_at,
   281→                lock=lock,
   282→                clock_fn=clock_fn,
   283→            )
   284→        return
   285→
   286→    while pending:
   287→        try:
   288→            future = next(as_completed(pending, timeout=heartbeat))
   289→        except FuturesTimeoutError:
   290→            _heartbeat(
   291→                pending,
   292→                futures,
   293→                started_at,
   294→                lock,
   295→                indexes,
   296→                progress_fn,
   297→                clock_fn,
   298→                error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   299→                contract_cache=contract_cache,
   300→            )
   301→            continue
   302→        pending.discard(future)
   303→        _complete_parallel_future(
   304→            future=future,
   305→            futures=futures,
   306→            progress_fn=progress_fn,
   307→            error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
   308→            contract_cache=contract_cache,
   309→            failures=failures,
   310→            progress_failures=progress_failures,
   311→            started_at=started_at,
   312→            lock=lock,
   313→            clock_fn=clock_fn,
   314→        )
   315→
   316→
   317→def _heartbeat(
   318→    pending,
   319→    futures,
   320→    started_at,
   321→    lock,
   322→    indexes,
   323→    progress_fn,
   324→    clock_fn,
   325→    *,
   326→    error_log_fn=None,
   327→    contract_cache: dict[int, str] | None = None,
   328→):
   329→    """Build and emit a heartbeat with active/queued batch status."""
   330→    with lock:
   331→        active = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] in started_at)
   332→    active_set = set(active)
   333→    queued = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] not in active_set)
   334→    elapsed = {
   335→        idx: int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - started_at.get(idx, clock_fn())))
   336→        for idx in active
   337→    }
   338→    heartbeat_error = _emit_progress(
   339→        progress_fn,
   340→        -1,
   341→        "heartbeat",
   342→        None,
   343→        details={
   344→            "active_batches": active,
   345→            "queued_batches": queued,
   346→            "elapsed_seconds": elapsed,
   347→            "active_count": len(active),
   348→            "queued_count": len(queued),
   349→            "total_count": len(indexes),
```

```
1→"""Assessment import policy and provenance helpers for review imports."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from dataclasses import replace
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.intelligence.review.importing.contracts import (
    10→    AssessmentImportPolicy,
    11→    AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
    12→    AssessmentProvenanceModel,
    13→    ReviewImportPayload,

    15→
    16→from ..runtime_paths import blind_packet_path, runtime_project_root
    17→
    18→[REDACTED]
    19→BLIND_PROVENANCE_KIND = "blind_review_batch_import"
    20→SUPPORTED_BLIND_REVIEW_RUNNERS = {"codex", "claude"}
    21→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_RUNNERS = {"claude"}
    22→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_REQUIRED_PHRASES = ("without awareness", "unbiased")
    23→ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_ATTEST_EXAMPLE = (
    24→    "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."
    25→)
    26→ASSESSMENT_MODE_LABELS = {
    27→    "none": "issues-only (no assessments in payload)",
    28→    "trusted_internal": "trusted internal (durable scores)",
    29→    "attested_external": "attested external (durable scores)",
    30→    "manual_override": "manual override (provisional scores)",
    31→    "issues_only": "issues-only (assessments skipped)",
    32→}
    33→
    34→
    35→def _default_blind_packet_path() -> Path:
    36→    return blind_packet_path()
    37→
    38→
    39→def _is_sha256_hex(raw: object) -> bool:
    40→    return (
    41→        isinstance(raw, str)
    42→        and len(raw) == 64
    43→        and all(ch in "0123456789abcdefABCDEF" for ch in raw)
    44→    )
    45→
    46→
    47→def _hash_file_sha256(path: Path) -> str | None:
    48→    try:
    49→        data = path.read_bytes()
    50→    except OSError:
    51→        return None
    52→    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()
    53→
    54→
    55→def _resolve_packet_path(raw_path: object) -> Path | None:
    56→    if not isinstance(raw_path, str):
    57→        return None
    58→    text = raw_path.strip()
    59→    if not text:
    60→        return None
    61→    path = Path(text)
    62→    return path if path.is_absolute() else runtime_project_root() / path
    63→
    64→
    65→def _assessment_provenance_status(
    66→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
    67→    *,
    68→    import_file: str,
    69→) -> AssessmentProvenanceModel:
    70→    """Evaluate whether assessments come from a trusted blind batch artifact."""
    71→    provenance = issues_data["provenance"]
    72→    if not provenance:
    73→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    74→            trusted=False,
    75→            reason="missing provenance metadata",
    76→            import_file=import_file,
    77→        )
    78→
    79→    kind = str(provenance.get("kind", "")).strip()
    80→    if kind != BLIND_PROVENANCE_KIND:
    81→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    82→            trusted=False,
    83→            reason=f"unsupported provenance kind: {kind or '<missing>'}",
    84→            import_file=import_file,
    85→        )
    86→
    87→    if provenance.get("blind") is not True:
    88→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    89→            trusted=False,
    90→            reason="provenance is not marked blind=true",
    91→            import_file=import_file,
    92→        )
    93→
    94→    runner = str(provenance.get("runner", "")).strip().lower()
    95→    if runner not in SUPPORTED_BLIND_REVIEW_RUNNERS:
    96→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
    97→            trusted=False,
    98→            reason=f"unsupported runner in provenance: {runner or '<missing>'}",
    99→            import_file=import_file,
   100→        )
   101→
   102→    packet_hash = provenance.get("packet_sha256")
   103→    if not _is_sha256_hex(packet_hash):
   104→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   105→            trusted=False,
   106→            reason="missing or invalid packet_sha256 in provenance",
   107→            import_file=import_file,
   108→        )
   109→
   110→    packet_path = _resolve_packet_path(provenance.get("packet_path"))
   111→    if packet_path is None:
   112→        packet_path = _default_blind_packet_path()
   113→    if not packet_path.exists():
   114→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   115→            trusted=False,
   116→            reason=f"blind packet not found: {packet_path}",
   117→            import_file=import_file,
   118→        )
   119→    observed_hash = _hash_file_sha256(packet_path)
   120→    if observed_hash is None:
   121→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   122→            trusted=False,
   123→            reason=f"unable to hash blind packet: {packet_path}",
   124→            import_file=import_file,
   125→        )
   126→    if observed_hash != packet_hash:
   127→        return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   128→            trusted=False,
   129→            reason=(
   130→                "blind packet hash mismatch "
   131→                f"(expected {packet_hash[:12]}..., got {observed_hash[:12]}...)"
   132→            ),
   133→            import_file=import_file,
   134→        )
   135→
   136→    return AssessmentProvenanceModel(
   137→        trusted=True,
   138→        reason="trusted blind subagent provenance",
   139→        runner=runner,
   140→        packet_path=str(packet_path),
   141→        packet_sha256=packet_hash,
   142→        import_file=import_file,
   143→    )
   144→
   145→
   146→def validate_attested_external_attestation(attest: str | None) -> str | None:
   147→    """Validate and normalize attestation text for attested external imports."""
   148→    if not isinstance(attest, str) or not attest.strip():
   149→        return None
   150→    text = attest.strip()
   151→    lowered = text.lower()
   152→    if all(phrase in lowered for phrase in ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_REQUIRED_PHRASES):
   153→        return text
   154→    return None
   155→
   156→
   157→def apply_assessment_import_policy(
   158→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   159→    *,
   160→    import_file: str,
   161→    attested_external: bool,
   162→    attested_attest: str | None,
   163→    manual_override: bool,
   164→    manual_attest: str | None,
   165→    trusted_assessment_source: bool,
   166→    trusted_assessment_label: str | None,
   167→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   168→    """Apply trust gating for assessment imports (issues import always allowed)."""
   169→    assessments = issues_data["assessments"]
   170→    has_assessments = bool(assessments)
   171→    assessment_count = len(assessments) if has_assessments else 0
   172→    provenance_status = _assessment_provenance_status(
   173→        issues_data, import_file=import_file
   174→    )
   175→    policy = AssessmentImportPolicyModel(
   176→        assessments_present=has_assessments,
   177→        assessment_count=int(assessment_count),
   178→        trusted=False,
   179→        mode="none",
   180→        reason="",
   181→        provenance=provenance_status,
   182→    )
   183→
   184→    if not has_assessments:
   185→        return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, policy), []
   186→
   187→    if trusted_assessment_source:
   188→        trusted_policy = replace(
   189→            policy,
   190→            mode="trusted_internal",
   191→            trusted=True,
   192→            reason=(trusted_assessment_label or "trusted internal run-batches import"),
   193→        )
   194→        return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, trusted_policy), []
   195→
   196→    if attested_external:
   197→        return _apply_attested_external_policy(
   198→            issues_data,
   199→            policy=policy,
   200→            provenance_status=provenance_status,
   201→            attested_attest=attested_attest,
   202→        )
   203→
   204→    if manual_override:
   205→        return _apply_manual_override_policy(
   206→            issues_data,
   207→            policy=policy,
   208→            manual_attest=manual_attest,
   209→        )
   210→
   211→    return _apply_issues_only_policy(
   212→        issues_data,
   213→        policy=policy,
   214→        provenance_status=provenance_status,
   215→    )
   216→
   217→
   218→def _attach_assessment_policy(
   219→    payload: ReviewImportPayload,
   220→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   221→) -> ReviewImportPayload:
   222→    normalized = dict(payload)
   223→    normalized[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = policy.to_dict()
   224→    return normalized
   225→
   226→
   227→def _apply_attested_external_policy(
   228→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   229→    *,
   230→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   231→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   232→    attested_attest: str | None,
   233→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   234→    normalized_attest = validate_attested_external_attestation(attested_attest)
   235→    if normalized_attest is None:
   236→        return None, [
   237→            "--attested-external requires --attest containing both "
   238→            "'without awareness' and 'unbiased'"
   239→        ]
   240→    if provenance_status.trusted is not True:
   241→        return None, [
   242→            "--attested-external requires valid blind packet provenance "
   243→            f"(current status: {provenance_status.reason or 'untrusted provenance'})"
   244→        ]
   245→    runner = provenance_status.runner.strip().lower()
   246→    if runner not in ATTESTED_EXTERNAL_RUNNERS:
   247→        return None, [
   248→            "--attested-external currently supports runner='claude' provenance only"
   249→        ]
   250→    attested_policy = replace(
   251→        policy,
   252→        mode="attested_external",
   253→        trusted=True,
   254→        reason="attested external blind subagent provenance",
   255→        attest=normalized_attest,
   256→    )
   257→    return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, attested_policy), []
   258→
   259→
   260→def _apply_manual_override_policy(
   261→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   262→    *,
   263→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   264→    manual_attest: str | None,
   265→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload | None, list[str]]:
   266→    if not isinstance(manual_attest, str) or not manual_attest.strip():
   267→        return None, ["--manual-override requires --attest"]
   268→    override_policy = replace(
   269→        policy,
   270→        mode="manual_override",
   271→        reason="manual override attested by operator",
   272→        attest=manual_attest.strip(),
   273→    )
   274→    return _attach_assessment_policy(issues_data, override_policy), []
   275→
   276→
   277→def _issues_only_reason(
   278→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   279→    *,
   280→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   281→) -> str:
   282→    if not issues_data["provenance"]:
   283→        return "missing trusted run-batches source; imported issues only"
   284→    provenance_reason = provenance_status.reason.strip()
   285→    if provenance_status.trusted is True:
   286→        return (
   287→            "external imports cannot self-attest trust even when provenance appears valid; "
   288→            "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   289→        )
   290→    if provenance_reason:
   291→        return (
   292→            "external imports cannot self-attest trust "
   293→            f"({provenance_reason}); run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   294→        )
   295→    return (
   296→        "external imports cannot self-attest trust; "
   297→        "run review --run-batches to apply assessments automatically"
   298→    )
   299→
   300→
   301→def _apply_issues_only_policy(
   302→    issues_data: ReviewImportPayload,
   303→    *,
   304→    policy: AssessmentImportPolicyModel,
   305→    provenance_status: AssessmentProvenanceModel,
   306→) -> tuple[ReviewImportPayload, list[str]]:
   307→    issues_only_policy = replace(
   308→        policy,
   309→        mode="issues_only",
   310→        reason=_issues_only_reason(issues_data, provenance_status=provenance_status),
   311→    )
   312→    payload = dict(issues_data)
   313→    payload["assessments"] = {}
   314→    payload[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY] = issues_only_policy.to_dict()
   315→    return payload, []
   316→
   317→
   318→def assessment_policy_from_payload(payload: ReviewImportPayload) -> AssessmentImportPolicy:
   319→    """Return parsed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   320→    policy = payload[ASSESSMENT_POLICY_KEY]
   321→    if isinstance(policy, dict):
   322→        return policy
   323→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel().to_dict()
   324→
   325→
   326→def assessment_policy_model_from_payload(
   327→    payload: ReviewImportPayload,
   328→) -> AssessmentImportPolicyModel:
   329→    """Return typed assessment policy metadata from a loaded import payload."""
   330→    return AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(assessment_policy_from_payload(payload))
   331→
   332→
   333→def assessment_mode_label(policy: AssessmentImportPolicy) -> str:
   334→    """Return a user-facing label for the selected assessment import mode."""
   335→    mode = AssessmentImportPolicyModel.from_mapping(policy).mode.strip().lower()
   336→    return ASSESSMENT_MODE_LABELS.get(mode, f"unknown ({mode or 'none'})")
   337→
```

Perfect. Now I have all the information I need to provide a comprehensive trace of the batch review execution flow. Let me compile my findings.

## Complete Batch Review Execution Flow

Based on my thorough examination of the codebase, here's the end-to-end flow:

### **1. ENTRY POINT: `do_run_batches()` in orchestrator.py (Line 181)**

This is the main orchestrator that delegates to the execution module. It sets up:
- Runtime policy (timeouts, retries, parallelism) → `resolve_batch_run_policy(args)` (line 186)
- Dependency injection functions for all sub-operations
- Delegates to `review_batches_mod.do_run_batches()` (line 228)

---

### **2. EXECUTION SETUP: `do_run_batches()` in execution.py (Line 915)**

This is the core state machine that orchestrates the entire batch pipeline:

#### **Phase 1: Prepare Run Setup** (`_prepare_run_setup()` lines 700-765)
1. **Validate runner**: `validate_runner(runner, colorize_fn)` (line 713)
2. **Load or prepare packet**:
   - Either loads packet from file override (`_load_or_prepare_packet()` line 718) or
   - Calls `review_mod.prepare_holistic_review()` to generate fresh packet
   - Returns: `packet`, `immutable_packet_path`, `prompt_packet_path` (blind packet)
3. **Parse packet metadata**:
   - Dimensions list (line 727)
   - Batch list with file groups (line 736)
4. **Select batch indexes**: 
   - `selected_batch_indexes_fn()` parses `--only-batches` arg (lines 741)
   - Returns 0-based indexes of batches to run

#### **Phase 2: Prepare Execution State** (`_prepare_execution_state()` lines 768-912)
1. **Calculate runtime expectations**:
   - `_runtime_expectation()` (line 779) → returns `(effective_workers, worst_case_minutes)`
2. **Call `prepare_run_artifacts_fn()`** → `prepare_run_artifacts()` in runner_packets.py (line 792)
   - **Creates directory structure**:
     - `{run_root}/{stamp}/prompts/` → prompt `.md` files
     - `{run_root}/{stamp}/results/` → batch output `.raw.txt` files
     - `{run_root}/{stamp}/logs/` → batch execution logs
   - **For each selected batch index**:
     - Calls `build_prompt_fn()` → `batch_core_mod.build_batch_prompt()` (line 173)
     - Which calls `render_batch_prompt()` from prompt_template.py (line 705)
     - **Writes to**: `prompts_dir/batch-{idx+1}.md`
   - Returns: `(run_dir, logs_dir, prompt_files, output_files, log_files)` dicts
3. **Build batch tasks**:
   - `build_batch_tasks()` in batches_runtime.py (line 834)
   - Creates dict of `{batch_idx: partial(_run_batch_task, ...)}` (line 334)
   - Each partial is bound to: `prompt_path`, `output_path`, `log_path`, `project_root`
4. **Build tracking structures**:
   - `_build_batch_tracking()` (line 842) → creates `batch_positions`, `batch_status` dicts
5. **Build progress reporter**:
   - `_build_progress_reporter()` (line 857) → wraps `_handle_batch_progress()` and `_handle_heartbeat_progress()`
6. **Dry-run check**: `_handle_dry_run()` (line 824) → returns `None` to skip execution if `--dry-run`

---

### **3. BATCH EXECUTION: `_execute_and_collect_results()` (Lines 456-507)**

#### **Run batches in parallel or serial**:
- Calls `execute_batches_fn()` → `execute_batches()` in runner_parallel.py (line 33)

**If parallel** (`run_parallel=True`):
1. `_resolve_parallel_runtime()` (line 49) → calc `max_workers = min(batch_count, requested)`
2. `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers)` (line 58)
3. `_queue_parallel_tasks()` (line 59):
   - For each batch, emit "queued" progress event (line 153)
   - Submit `_run_parallel_task()` to executor (line 171)
4. `_drain_parallel_completions()` (line 74):
   - Monitors futures with optional heartbeat timeout
   - If heartbeat fires, `_heartbeat()` emits active/queued batch status (lines 317-355)
   - Completes futures: `_complete_parallel_future()` (line 272)

**If serial** (`run_parallel=False`):
- `_execute_serial()` (line 89):
  - For each batch: emit "start", run `tasks[idx]()`, emit "done"

#### **Per-batch execution**: `_run_parallel_task()` / serial equivalent
- Calls `tasks[idx]()` → `_run_batch_task()` in batches_runtime.py (line 400)

**Inside `_run_batch_task()`** (batches_runtime.py:400-420):
1. Read prompt text from file: `prompt_path.read_text()` (line 410)
2. Call `run_codex_batch_fn()` → `run_codex_batch()` in runner_process.py (line 47)

**Inside `run_codex_batch()`** (runner_process.py:47-121):
1. Build command: `codex_batch_command()` (line 24)
   - Reads `DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT` env var
   - Returns: `["codex", "exec", "--ephemeral", "-C", repo_root, ..., "-o", output_file, prompt]`
2. Retry loop (lines 67-118):
   - `_run_batch_attempt()` → executes subprocess with timeout/stall handling
   - `_handle_early_attempt_return()` → check for immediate success
   - `_handle_timeout_or_stall()` → handle timeout/stall, return early if needed
   - `_handle_successful_attempt()` → if exit code 0, return 0
   - `_handle_failed_attempt()` → if failure, retry or return non-zero
3. Returns exit code (0 = success, non-zero = failure)

#### **Result collection**: `collect_batch_results_fn()` (line 501)
- Calls `collect_batch_results()` in runner_parallel.py (line 99)
- For each selected batch:
  1. If execution succeeded, try to read output: `raw_path.read_text()` (line 118)
  2. Call `extract_payload_fn()` → `batch_core_mod.extract_json_payload()` (line 217)
     - Searches for first valid `{assessments: {...}, issues: [...]}` JSON object
  3. If no file output, try `_extract_payload_from_log()` to parse from log file
  4. Call `normalize_result_fn()` → `batch_core_mod.normalize_batch_result()` (line 218)
     - Validates assessments dict against allowed dimensions
     - Validates dimension_notes (evidence, impact_scope, fix_scope, confidence)
     - Validates and caps issues array (max issues per dimension count)
     - Enforces low-score dimensions have explicit issues
     - Returns: `(assessments, issues, dimension_notes, quality)` tuple
  5. Append to `batch_results` list if successful

---

### **4. FAILURE HANDLING & MERGE**

#### **Update batch statuses**: `_update_batch_statuses()` (line 981)
- Marks successful/failed/parse_failed/missing_output status per batch

#### **Handle failures**: `_handle_failures_before_import()` (line 993)
- If `allow_partial=False` and failures exist → raise via `print_failures_and_raise_fn()`
- If `allow_partial=True` → print warnings, continue

#### **Merge batch results**: `_merge_and_write_payload()` (line 1006)
1. Call `merge_batch_results_fn()` → `batch_core_mod.merge_batch_results()` in merge.py (line 133)
   
   **Inside `merge_batch_results()`** (merge.py:133-213):
   - For each batch result:
     - `_accumulate_batch_scores()` → collect scores per dimension with weights
     - `_accumulate_batch_quality()` → track coverage/evidence metrics
     - `_append_batch_issues()` → collect all issues into flat list
   - `_merge_issues_transitively()` (line 167):
     - Groups issues by identity key
     - For each group, applies `_merge_issue_group()` using connected components
     - Merges on: identifier match, summary similarity (≥45% word overlap), file overlap
     - Transitive merge: A≈B and B≈C → A merged with C
   - Compute merged assessments from weighted score buckets
   - Return: `{assessments, dimension_notes, issues, review_quality}`

2. Collect reviewed files: `collect_reviewed_files_from_batches()` (line 572)
3. Build review_scope metadata (line 584):
   - `reviewed_files_count`
   - `successful_batch_count`
   - `full_sweep_included`
   - `total_files` (if known)
4. Build provenance: `build_import_provenance_fn()` → `build_batch_import_provenance()` in runner_packets.py (line 103)
   - Returns: `{kind, blind, runner, run_stamp, batch_count, batch_indexes, packet_sha256}`
5. Compute assessment coverage and print notices
6. Write merged payload: `safe_write_text_fn(merged_path, ...)` (line 637)
   - File: `{run_dir}/holistic_issues_merged.json`

---

### **5. IMPORT INTO STATE** 

#### **Import merged payload**: `_import_merged_payload()` (line 1023)
1. Call `do_import_fn()` → `_do_import()` from importing/cmd.py
   - Trusted source (`trusted_assessment_source=True`)
   - Merges assessments/issues into `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json`
   - Updates finding history
2. Log completion: `append_run_log()` → writes to `run.log`

#### **Optional follow-up scan**: `_run_followup_scan_if_requested()` (line 1035)
- If `--scan-after-import`, runs `desloppify scan` subprocess
- Timeout: `FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 45 * 60` (line 57)

---

### **6. FILE STRUCTURE CREATED**

```
{subagent_runs_dir}/{stamp}/
├── run_summary.json              # run metadata & batch statuses
├── run.log                        # execution log with timestamps
├── holistic_issues_merged.json    # final merged assessments & issues
├── prompts/
│   ├── batch-1.md                # codex prompt for batch 1
│   ├── batch-2.md
│   └── batch-N.md
├── results/
│   ├── batch-1.raw.txt           # codex raw output (JSON)
│   ├── batch-2.raw.txt
│   └── batch-N.raw.txt
└── logs/
    ├── batch-1.log               # codex stderr/stdout
    ├── batch-2.log
    └── batch-N.log
```

---

### **7. KEY INJECTION POINTS & FUNCTION SIGNATURES**

| Function | File | Line | Purpose |
|----------|------|------|---------|
| `do_run_batches()` | orchestrator.py | 181 | Main entry point, dependency injection |
| `do_run_batches()` | execution.py | 915 | Core orchestration state machine |
| `prepare_run_artifacts()` | runner_packets.py | 143 | Generate prompts & create directory structure |
| `build_batch_prompt()` | batch/core.py | 697 | Delegates to `render_batch_prompt()` |
| `render_batch_prompt()` | batch/prompt_template.py | 388 | Constructs full subagent prompt text |
| `build_batch_tasks()` | batches_runtime.py | 324 | Creates partial task callables |
| `_run_batch_task()` | batches_runtime.py | 400 | Reads prompt, calls `run_codex_batch()` |
| `run_codex_batch()` | runner_process.py | 47 | Subprocess execution with retry/timeout |
| `codex_batch_command()` | runner_process.py | 24 | Builds `codex exec` command line |
| `execute_batches()` | runner_parallel.py | 33 | Serial or parallel executor |
| `collect_batch_results()` | runner_parallel.py | 99 | Parses & normalizes JSON outputs |
| `extract_json_payload()` | batch/core.py | 131 | Best-effort JSON extraction |
| `normalize_batch_result()` | batch/core.py | 385 | Validates structure & caps issues |
| `merge_batch_results()` | batch/merge.py | 133 | Merges scores/issues transitively |
| `build_batch_import_provenance()` | runner_packets.py | 103 | Creates trusted source metadata |
| `_do_import()` | importing/cmd.py | — | Final state merge (imported from elsewhere) |

---

### **8. CRITICAL LOOPS & CALLBACKS**

**Progress events** flow through:
- `_emit_progress()` (runner_parallel_progress.py) → `progress_fn(BatchProgressEvent(...))` 
- Callbacks: "queued", "start", "done", "heartbeat"
- Handler: `_build_progress_reporter()` → `_handle_batch_progress()` or `_handle_heartbeat_progress()`

**Error handling**:
- Execution failures tracked in `failures: set[int]`
- Parse failures added to failure set in `collect_batch_results()`
- Raised via `print_failures_and_raise_fn()` unless `allow_partial=True`

This completes the entire batch review execution pipeline from `do_run_batches()` entry through final state import.

### **6. KEY SCHEMA DIFFERENCES: EXTERNAL vs BATCH**

Both flows use the **same `ReviewImportPayload` contract** (contracts_types.py lines 78-87).

**External pathway specifics:**
- Provenance is **canonical** (injected by CLI in `_canonical_external_payload()`)
  - Kind: `"blind_review_batch_import"` (not `"review_batch_import"`)
  - `blind: true` (marks it as external)
  - `runner: "claude"` (runner from session)
  - `session_id`: links to external session
  - `packet_path` / `packet_sha256`: immutable packet metadata

- Assessment policy mode: `"attested_external"` (only valid with `--attested-external` flag)
- `manual_attest`: persisted from session's EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT

**Both support:**
- `dimension_notes`: dict of `{dimension: {evidence: [strings]}}`
- `reviewed_files`: list of scanned file paths
- `review_scope`: metadata (full_sweep_included flag)

---

### **7. CRITICAL FLOW DECISION POINT**

**Line 398 in cmd.py is the unified import bottleneck:**

```python
diff = review_mod.import_holistic_issues(issues_data, working_state, lang.name)
```

This is called **identically** for:
- Batch review imports (from `--run-batches`)
- External review imports (from `--external-submit`)
- Manual override imports

The **only difference** is the provenance metadata and assessment policy mode, which are already baked into `issues_data` by the time it reaches this call.

**For external flow specifically**, the provenance with `packet_sha256` and `session_id` is what enables:
1. **Trust verification**: Policy layer can validate blind packet hash (policy.py lines 102-126)
2. **Audit trail**: Session metadata persisted linking external_reviewer_output.json → canonical_import.json → state update
3. **Durable scoring**: Mode `"attested_external"` with valid provenance bypasses provisional flags

---

### **8. FILE LOCATIONS SUMMARY**

**Session directory:** `_external_session_root() / session_id`
- `session.json` - metadata, paths, expiry, token
- `review_result.template.json` - template for reviewer
- `claude_launch_prompt.md` - blind reviewer prompt
- `reviewer_instructions.md` - instructions
- `review_result.json` - reviewer's output (written by reviewer)
- `canonical_import_{stamp}.json` - canonicalized import file (written by CLI)

**Packet directory:** `.desloppify/review_packets/`
- `review_packet_{stamp}.json` - immutable full packet
- `blind_packet_sha256_{hash}.json` - blind packet (no scores/targets)

**State:**
- `.desloppify/state-{lang}.json` - updated state with issues and assessments
- `.desloppify/query.json` - query output with narrative/scores

I need to design a plan to align the two review paths (batch and external) so they both deliver the same context to the reviewer. Here's what I've learned:

## Current State

**Batch path:**
1. Creates blind packet (system_prompt, dimension_prompts, investigation_batches, holistic_context)
2. For each selected batch, generates a rich prompt via `render_batch_prompt()` in prompt_template.py
3. Passes prompt to `codex exec` — subagent reads blind packet from disk
4. Each batch output: `{batch, batch_index, assessments, dimension_notes, issues, retrospective}`
5. Results merged by `merge_batch_results()` in merge.py
6. Merged payload imported via `do_import()`

**External path:**
1. Creates SAME blind packet (same function)
2. Generates bare-bones 8-line prompt in `_build_claude_launch_prompt()` in external.py
3. Writes to markdown file for copy/paste into a Claude session
4. Single output: `{session, assessments, issues}` — no dimension_notes, no per-batch structure
5. CLI wraps with canonical provenance in `_canonical_external_payload()`
6. Imported via same `do_import()`

**Key gap:** External path doesn't use `render_batch_prompt()`. Its launch prompt never mentions system_prompt, scoring philosophy, dimension_notes, scoring frame, seed files, etc. The reviewer gets almost no guidance compared to batch subagents.

## Design Constraints

1. Both paths should show the reviewer the same information and use the same scoring rules
2. The blind packet already contains `investigation_batches` — same data is available
3. Batch path runs N parallel codex subagents, one per batch
4. External path is ONE manual Claude session — the reviewer can't easily produce N separate outputs
5. The submit flow (`do_external_submit`) expects a single JSON with `session` key
6. The import pipeline already handles both — it just needs assessments and issues
7. `render_batch_prompt` generates ONE prompt per batch — it can't directly generate one combined prompt

## My Proposed Approach

**Rewrite `_build_claude_launch_prompt` to:**
1. Include session metadata header (token, paths) — keep this
2. Include the same scoring frame from the batch prompt ("YOUR TASK" / "WHAT WE GIVE YOU AND WHY")
3. Reference the blind packet and tell reviewer to follow `system_prompt` constraints (same as batch)
4. Include ALL batch information from the packet: for each batch, list dimensions, seed files, rationale, historical issues, concern signals — essentially a combined view of what batch subagents each get individually
5. Use the full output schema including dimension_notes (currently missing from external)
6. Keep the session wrapper requirement (session.id, session.token)

This means `_build_claude_launch_prompt` would need access to the packet data (to extract batches). Currently it only gets session_id, token, and file paths.

**What changes:**
- `_build_claude_launch_prompt` signature changes to accept `packet` dict (or just the batches + blind_path)
- The function iterates `investigation_batches` from the packet and includes their details
- Reuses `_render_scoring_frame()`, `_render_scan_evidence_note()`, etc. from prompt_template.py — or calls into a shared rendering layer
- Output schema includes dimension_notes
- Template payload updated to include dimension_notes: {}

**What stays the same:**
- Session creation, token, expiry, provenance — all unchanged
- Submit flow (`do_external_submit`) — works because import pipeline already handles dimension_notes
- `_canonical_external_payload` — unchanged
- The import pipeline — unchanged
- Blind packet creation — unchanged

**Alternative I considered:** Make external path generate N separate prompt files and submit N separate outputs. Rejected because it fundamentally changes the external UX from "one session" to "N sessions" which is a bigger refactor and worse for the manual copy/paste workflow.

**Question:** Should the external prompt reuse render functions from prompt_template.py directly? Or should we extract shared rendering into a common module? prompt_template.py is currently batch-specific (it assumes one batch per prompt). We'd need either:
A) Extract shared functions (scoring frame, scan evidence note, historical focus, concern signals) into a shared module, used by both prompt_template.py and external.py
B) Call render_batch_prompt for each batch and concatenate them into one document with a session header
C) Build the external prompt independently but using the same patterns/text

Option A is cleanest. Option B means the external reviewer sees N output schemas (confusing). Option C duplicates text.

Please evaluate this design, identify any issues, and suggest the cleanest implementation approach. Consider:
- What shared rendering functions should be extracted?
- What's the right function signature for the new `_build_claude_launch_prompt`?
- Does the template payload need updating?
- Are there any issues with the submit/import flow accepting dimension_notes from external?

```
   334→        )

   336→    print(colorize(f"  2. Reviewer output target: `{output_path}`", "dim"))
   337→    print(colorize(f"  3. Submit results: `{submit_cmd}`", "dim"))
   338→    print(colorize(f"  4. Optional auto-rescan: `{submit_with_scan_cmd}`", "dim"))
   339→
   340→
   341→def _canonical_external_payload(
   342→    raw_payload: dict[str, Any],
   343→    *,
   344→    session: dict[str, Any],
   345→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   346→    """Return import payload with canonical provenance and required session token."""
   347→    session_meta = raw_payload.get("session")
   348→    if not isinstance(session_meta, dict):
   349→        raise CommandError(
   350→            "Error: external reviewer payload must include top-level `session` object."
   351→            ' Expected: {"session":{"id":"...","token":"..."},"assessments":{...},"issues":[...]}',
   352→        )
   353→
   354→    payload_id = str(session_meta.get("id", "")).strip()
   355→    payload_token = str(session_meta.get("token", "")).strip()
   356→    expected_id = str(session.get("session_id", "")).strip()
   357→    expected_token = str(session.get("token", "")).strip()
   358→    if payload_id != expected_id or payload_token != expected_token:
   359→        raise CommandError(
   360→            "Error: session id/token mismatch in external reviewer payload."
   361→            " Regenerate output using the session template/instructions.",
   362→        )
   363→
   364→    payload = {
   365→        key: value
   366→        for key, value in raw_payload.items()
   367→        if key not in {"session", "provenance"}
   368→    }
   369→    payload["provenance"] = {
   370→        "kind": "blind_review_batch_import",
   371→        "blind": True,
   372→        "runner": str(session.get("runner", "claude")),
   373→        "session_id": str(session.get("session_id", "")),
   374→        "created_at": _iso_seconds(_utc_now()),
   375→        "packet_path": str(session.get("blind_packet_path", "")),
   376→        "packet_sha256": str(session.get("packet_sha256", "")),
   377→    }
   378→    return payload
   379→
   380→
   381→def _ensure_session_open(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   382→    status = str(session.get("status", "open")).strip().lower()
   383→    if status == "open":
   384→        return
   385→    raise CommandError(
   386→        f"Error: session is not open (status={status or 'unknown'}). Start a new session with --external-start.",
   387→    )
   388→
   389→
   390→def _ensure_session_not_expired(session: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   391→    expires_at = _parse_iso(session.get("expires_at"))
   392→    if expires_at is None:
   393→        raise CommandError(
   394→            "Error: session metadata is missing/invalid expires_at.",
   395→        )
   396→    now = _utc_now()
   397→    if now <= expires_at:
   398→        return
   399→    raise CommandError(
   400→        f"Error: session expired at {session.get('expires_at')}. Start a new session with --external-start.",
   401→    )
   402→
   403→
   404→def do_external_submit(
   405→    *,
   406→    import_file: str,
   407→    session_id: str,
   408→    state: dict,
   409→    lang,
   410→    state_file,
   411→    config: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   412→    allow_partial: bool = False,
   413→    scan_after_import: bool = False,
   414→    scan_path: str = ".",
   415→    dry_run: bool = False,
   416→) -> None:
   417→    """Submit external reviewer output via session, adding canonical provenance."""
   418→    config = config or {}
   419→    session_path, session = _session_payload(session_id)
   420→    _ensure_session_open(session)
   421→    _ensure_session_not_expired(session)
   422→
   423→    if str(session.get("runner", "")).strip().lower() not in _EXTERNAL_SUPPORTED_RUNNERS:
   424→        raise CommandError(
   425→            "Error: only Claude external sessions currently support durable score submit.",
   426→        )
   427→
   428→    issues_path = Path(import_file)
   429→    raw_payload = _load_json_object(issues_path, label="external issues")
   430→    canonical_payload = _canonical_external_payload(raw_payload, session=session)
   431→
   432→    stamp = run_stamp()
   433→    session_dir = session_path.parent
   434→    canonical_path = session_dir / f"canonical_import_{stamp}.json"
   435→    safe_write_text(canonical_path, json.dumps(canonical_payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   436→
   437→    if dry_run:
   438→        do_validate_import(
   439→            str(canonical_path),
   440→            lang,
   441→            allow_partial=allow_partial,
   442→            attested_external=True,
   443→            manual_attest=str(session.get("attest", EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT)),
   444→        )
   445→        return
   446→
   447→    do_import(
   448→        str(canonical_path),
   449→        state,
   450→        lang,
   451→        state_file,
   452→        config=config,
   453→        allow_partial=allow_partial,
   454→        attested_external=True,
   455→        manual_attest=str(session.get("attest", EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT)),
   456→    )
   457→
   458→    submitted_at = _iso_seconds(_utc_now())
   459→    session["status"] = "submitted"
   460→    session["submitted_at"] = submitted_at
   461→    session["submitted_input_file"] = str(issues_path)
   462→    session["submitted_canonical_file"] = str(canonical_path)
   463→    safe_write_text(session_path, json.dumps(session, indent=2) + "\n")
   464→
   465→    if scan_after_import:
   466→        code = run_followup_scan(
   467→            lang_name=lang.name,
   468→            scan_path=scan_path,
   469→            deps=FollowupScanDeps(
   470→                project_root=_runtime_project_root(),
   471→                timeout_seconds=FOLLOWUP_SCAN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS,
   472→                python_executable=sys.executable,
   473→                subprocess_run=subprocess.run,
   474→                timeout_error=subprocess.TimeoutExpired,
   475→                colorize_fn=colorize,
   476→            ),
   477→        )
   478→        if code != 0:
   479→            raise SystemExit(code)
   480→
   481→
   482→__all__ = ["do_external_start", "do_external_submit", "EXTERNAL_ATTEST_TEXT"]
   483→
```

```
    99→def _render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   100→    focus = batch.get("historical_issue_focus")
   101→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):
   102→        return ""
   103→
   104→    selected_raw = focus.get("selected_count", 0)
   105→    try:
   106→        selected_count = max(0, int(selected_raw))
   107→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   108→        selected_count = 0
   109→
   110→    issues = focus.get("issues", [])
   111→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
   112→        issues = []
   113→
   114→    if selected_count <= 0 or not issues:
   115→        return ""
   116→
   117→    lines: list[str] = []
   118→    lines.append(
   119→        "Previously flagged issues — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:"
   120→    )
   121→    lines.append(
   122→        "Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report"
   123→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"
   124→        " what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out."
   125→    )
   126→
   127→    for entry in issues:
   128→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   129→            continue
   130→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
   131→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   132→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
   133→
   134→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
   135→        if note:
   136→            line += f" (note: {note})"
   137→        lines.append(line)
   138→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   139→
   140→
   141→def _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   142→    """Render mechanically-generated concern hypotheses for this batch."""
   143→    signals = batch.get("concern_signals")
   144→    if not isinstance(signals, list) or not signals:
   145→        return ""
   146→
   147→    lines: list[str] = []
   148→    lines.append("Mechanical concern signals — navigation aid, not scoring evidence:")
   149→    lines.append(
   150→        "Confirm or refute each with your own code reading. Report only confirmed defects."
   151→    )
   152→
   153→    shown = 0
   154→    for entry in signals:
   155→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   156→            continue
   157→        file = str(entry.get("file", "")).strip() or "(unknown file)"
   158→        concern_type = str(entry.get("type", "")).strip() or "design_concern"
   159→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   160→        question = str(entry.get("question", "")).strip()
   161→        evidence_raw = entry.get("evidence", [])
   162→        evidence = (
   163→            [str(item).strip() for item in evidence_raw if isinstance(item, str) and item.strip()]
   164→            if isinstance(evidence_raw, list)
   165→            else []
   166→        )
   167→
   168→        lines.append(f"  - [{concern_type}] {file}")
   169→        if summary:
   170→            lines.append(f"    summary: {summary}")
   171→        if question:
   172→            lines.append(f"    question: {question}")
   173→        for snippet in evidence[:2]:
   174→            lines.append(f"    evidence: {snippet}")
   175→        shown += 1
   176→        if shown >= 8:
   177→            break
   178→
   179→    extra = max(0, len(signals) - shown)
   180→    if extra:
   181→        lines.append(f"  - (+{extra} more concern signals)")
   182→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   183→

   354→        '    "<dimension>": {\n'
   355→        '      "evidence": ["specific code observations"],\n'
   356→        '      "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   357→        '      "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   358→        '      "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   359→        f'      "{DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY}": "required when score >{HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD:.1f}",\n'
   360→        '      "sub_axes": {"abstraction_leverage": 0-100, "indirection_cost": 0-100, "interface_honesty": 0-100, "delegation_density": 0-100, "definition_directness": 0-100, "type_discipline": 0-100}  // required for abstraction_fitness when evidence supports it; all one decimal place\n'
   361→        "    }\n"
   362→        "  },\n"
   363→        '  "issues": [{\n'
   364→        '    "dimension": "<dimension>",\n'
   365→        '    "identifier": "short_id",\n'
   366→        '    "summary": "one-line defect summary",\n'
   367→        '    "related_files": ["relative/path.py"],\n'
   368→        '    "evidence": ["specific code observation"],\n'
   369→        '    "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",\n'
   370→        '    "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   371→        '    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   372→        '    "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   373→        '    "root_cause_cluster": "optional_cluster_name_when_supported_by_history"\n'
   374→        "  }],\n"
   375→        '  "retrospective": {\n'
   376→        '    "root_causes": ["optional: concise root-cause hypotheses"],\n'
   377→        '    "likely_symptoms": ["optional: identifiers that look symptom-level"],\n'
   378→        '    "possible_false_positives": ["optional: prior concept keys likely mis-scoped"]\n'
   379→        "  }\n"
   380→        "}\n"
   381→    )
   382→
   383→
   384→def _join_non_empty_sections(*sections: str) -> str:
   385→    return "".join(section for section in sections if section)
   386→
   387→
   388→def render_batch_prompt(
   389→    *,
   390→    repo_root: Path,
   391→    packet_path: Path,
   392→    batch_index: int,
   393→    batch: dict[str, object],
   394→) -> str:
   395→    """Render one subagent prompt for a holistic investigation batch."""
   396→    context = _build_context(batch, batch_index)
   397→    return _join_non_empty_sections(
   398→        _render_metadata_block(
   399→            repo_root=repo_root,
   400→            packet_path=packet_path,
   401→            batch_index=batch_index,
   402→            context=context,
   403→        ),
   404→        _render_scoring_frame(),
   405→        _render_scan_evidence_note(),
   406→        _render_seed_files_block(context),
   407→        _render_historical_focus(batch),
   408→        _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch),
   409→        _render_task_requirements(context),
   410→        _render_scope_enums(),
   411→        _render_output_schema(context, batch_index),
   412→    )
   413→
   414→
   415→__all__ = ["render_batch_prompt"]
   416→
```

```
1→"""Merge and dedupe logic for holistic review batch outputs."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import cast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
     8→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
     9→)
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.review.issue_merge import (
    11→    merge_list_fields,
    12→    normalize_word_set,
    13→    pick_longer_text,
    14→    track_merged_from,
    15→)
    16→
    17→from .core import (
    18→    BatchDimensionNotePayload,
    19→    BatchIssuePayload,
    20→    BatchResultPayload,
    21→    _accumulate_batch_quality,
    22→    _accumulate_batch_scores,
    23→    _compute_abstraction_components,
    24→    _compute_merged_assessments,
    25→    _issue_identity_key,
    26→    _issue_pressure_by_dimension,
    27→    assessment_weight,
    28→)
    29→
    30→
    31→def _merge_issue_payload(
    32→    existing: BatchIssuePayload,
    33→    incoming: BatchIssuePayload,
    34→) -> None:
    35→    merge_list_fields(existing, incoming, ("related_files", "evidence"))
    36→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "summary")
    37→    pick_longer_text(existing, incoming, "suggestion")
    38→    track_merged_from(existing, str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip())
    39→
    40→
    41→def _should_merge_issues(
    42→    existing: BatchIssuePayload,
    43→    incoming: BatchIssuePayload,
    44→) -> bool:
    45→    existing_summary = normalize_word_set(str(existing.get("summary", "")))
    46→    incoming_summary = normalize_word_set(str(incoming.get("summary", "")))
    47→    summary_similarity_signal = False
    48→    if existing_summary and incoming_summary:
    49→        overlap = len(existing_summary & incoming_summary)
    50→        union = len(existing_summary | incoming_summary)
    51→        summary_similarity_signal = bool(union and overlap / union >= 0.45)
    52→
    53→    existing_files = set(existing.get("related_files", []))
    54→    incoming_files = set(incoming.get("related_files", []))
    55→    file_overlap_signal = bool(existing_files and incoming_files and (existing_files & incoming_files))
    56→
    57→    existing_identifier = str(existing.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    58→    incoming_identifier = str(incoming.get("identifier", "")).strip()
    59→    identifier_signal = bool(
    60→        existing_identifier and incoming_identifier and existing_identifier == incoming_identifier
    61→    )
    62→
    63→    corroborating_signals = (
    64→        int(summary_similarity_signal)
    65→        + int(file_overlap_signal)
    66→        + int(identifier_signal)
    67→    )
    68→    if identifier_signal and (summary_similarity_signal or file_overlap_signal):
    69→        return True
    70→    return corroborating_signals >= 2
    71→
    72→
    73→def _append_batch_issues(
    74→    result: BatchResultPayload,
    75→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    76→) -> None:
    77→    for issue in result.get("issues", []):
    78→        if isinstance(issue, dict):
    79→            issues.append(cast(BatchIssuePayload, issue))
    80→
    81→
    82→def _merge_issue_group(group: list[BatchIssuePayload]) -> list[BatchIssuePayload]:
    83→    """Merge one dedupe-key group using transitive connected components."""
    84→    if len(group) <= 1:
    85→        return list(group)
    86→
    87→    visited: set[int] = set()
    88→    components: list[list[int]] = []
    89→
    90→    for start in range(len(group)):
    91→        if start in visited:
    92→            continue
    93→        stack = [start]
    94→        component: list[int] = []
    95→        visited.add(start)
    96→        while stack:
    97→            node = stack.pop()
    98→            component.append(node)
    99→            source = group[node]
   100→            for probe in range(len(group)):
   101→                if probe in visited:
   102→                    continue
   103→                target = group[probe]
   104→                if _should_merge_issues(source, target) or _should_merge_issues(
   105→                    target, source
   106→                ):
   107→                    visited.add(probe)
   108→                    stack.append(probe)
   109→        components.append(sorted(component))
   110→
   111→    merged_components: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   112→    for indexes in sorted(components, key=lambda ids: ids[0]):
   113→        base = group[indexes[0]]
   114→        for idx in indexes[1:]:
   115→            _merge_issue_payload(base, group[idx])
   116→        merged_components.append(base)
   117→    return merged_components
   118→
   119→
   120→def _merge_issues_transitively(
   121→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
   122→) -> list[BatchIssuePayload]:
   123→    grouped: dict[str, list[BatchIssuePayload]] = {}
   124→    for issue in issues:
   125→        grouped.setdefault(_issue_identity_key(issue), []).append(issue)
   126→
   127→    merged: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   128→    for group in grouped.values():
   129→        merged.extend(_merge_issue_group(group))
   130→    return merged
   131→
   132→
   133→def merge_batch_results(
   134→    batch_results: list[BatchResultPayload],
   135→    *,
   136→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
   137→    abstraction_component_names: dict[str, str],
   138→) -> dict[str, object]:
   139→    """Deterministically merge assessments/issues across batch outputs."""
   140→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {}
   141→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]] = {}
   142→    all_issues: list[BatchIssuePayload] = []
   143→    merged_dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload] = {}
   144→    coverage_values: list[float] = []
   145→    evidence_density_values: list[float] = []
   146→    high_score_missing_issue_note_total = 0.0
   147→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]] = {
   148→        axis: [] for axis in abstraction_sub_axes
   149→    }
   150→
   151→    for result in batch_results:
   152→        _accumulate_batch_scores(
   153→            result,
   154→            score_buckets=score_buckets,
   155→            score_raw_by_dim=score_raw_by_dim,
   156→            merged_dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   157→            abstraction_axis_scores=abstraction_axis_scores,
   158→            abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   159→        )
   160→        _append_batch_issues(result, all_issues)
   161→        high_score_missing_issue_note_total += _accumulate_batch_quality(
   162→            result,
   163→            coverage_values=coverage_values,
   164→            evidence_density_values=evidence_density_values,
   165→        )
   166→
   167→    merged_issues = _merge_issues_transitively(all_issues)
   168→    issue_pressure_by_dim, issue_count_by_dim = _issue_pressure_by_dimension(
   169→        merged_issues,
   170→        dimension_notes=merged_dimension_notes,
   171→    )
   172→
   173→    merged_assessments = _compute_merged_assessments(
   174→        score_buckets, score_raw_by_dim, issue_pressure_by_dim, issue_count_by_dim
   175→    )
   176→
   177→    merged_assessment_payload: dict[str, float | dict[str, object]] = {
   178→        key: value for key, value in merged_assessments.items()
   179→    }
   180→    component_scores = _compute_abstraction_components(
   181→        merged_assessments,
   182→        abstraction_axis_scores,
   183→        abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
   184→        abstraction_component_names=abstraction_component_names,
   185→    )
   186→    if component_scores is not None:
   187→        merged_assessment_payload["abstraction_fitness"] = {
   188→            "score": merged_assessments["abstraction_fitness"],
   189→            "components": list(component_scores),
   190→            "component_scores": component_scores,
   191→        }
   192→
   193→    return {
   194→        "assessments": merged_assessment_payload,
   195→        "dimension_notes": merged_dimension_notes,
   196→        "issues": merged_issues,
   197→        "review_quality": {
   198→            "batch_count": len(batch_results),
   199→            "dimension_coverage": round(
   200→                sum(coverage_values) / max(len(coverage_values), 1),
   201→                3,
   202→            ),
   203→            "evidence_density": round(
   204→                sum(evidence_density_values) / max(len(evidence_density_values), 1),
   205→                3,
   206→            ),
   207→            REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY: int(
   208→                high_score_missing_issue_note_total
   209→            ),
   210→            "issue_pressure": round(sum(issue_pressure_by_dim.values()), 3),
   211→            "dimensions_with_issues": len(issue_count_by_dim),
   212→        },
   213→    }
   214→
   215→
   216→__all__ = ["assessment_weight", "merge_batch_results"]
   217→
```
