## Design Evaluation and Implementation Plan

### Your analysis is correct on all key points

Your diagnosis of the gap is accurate: `_build_claude_launch_prompt` produces 8 bare lines while batch subagents get the full scoring frame, scan evidence guidance, seed files, historical issues, concern signals, dimension-specific focus instructions, and a detailed output schema. The import pipeline already handles `dimension_notes` as optional (defaults to `{}`), and `_canonical_external_payload` passes through all keys except `session` and `provenance`, so there is zero submit/import issue with adding `dimension_notes` to external output.

### Option A is the right call, with a specific refinement

You are correct to choose Option A (extract shared rendering functions). However, after reading the code, I would refine the approach: most of the `_render_*` functions in `prompt_template.py` are already pure functions that take either a `_PromptBatchContext` dataclass or a raw `batch` dict. They do not depend on batch-path-specific infrastructure. The extraction is lighter than it might seem.

Here is what I would specifically extract versus leave in place:

**Functions to extract into a new shared module** (e.g., `desloppify/app/commands/review/prompt_sections.py`):

1. `_render_scoring_frame()` -- takes no arguments, pure text
2. `_render_scan_evidence_note()` -- takes no arguments, pure text
3. `_render_historical_focus(batch)` -- takes a raw batch dict
4. `_render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)` -- takes a raw batch dict
5. `_render_scope_enums()` -- takes no arguments, pure text
6. `_SCAN_EVIDENCE_FOCUS_BY_DIMENSION` dict and `_render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)` -- takes a set
7. `_render_package_org_focus(dim_set)`, `_render_abstraction_focus(dim_set)`, `_render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set)` -- all take a set
8. `_render_dimension_focus(context)` -- takes a `_PromptBatchContext`; but the external path would call the underlying dim_set functions directly since it does not have per-batch contexts
9. `_coerce_string_list(raw)` -- utility
10. `_PromptBatchContext` dataclass -- used by both paths to structure batch data

**Functions to leave in `prompt_template.py`** (batch-path-specific composition):

1. `_render_metadata_block()` -- batch-specific header with batch_index, packet_path, repo_root
2. `_render_seed_files_block()` -- can be extracted but is trivial
3. `_render_task_requirements(context)` -- the detailed numbered requirements; this is the most interesting case (see below)
4. `_render_output_schema(context, batch_index)` -- output schema differs between paths
5. `render_batch_prompt()` -- the batch-path composition function

**The task requirements question:** `_render_task_requirements` contains the core scoring contract (rules 1-13). The external prompt needs the same rules, but rule 3 mentions `context.issues_cap` (per-batch cap). For the combined external prompt, you would want a total cap across all dimensions. I would extract the core requirements text into a shared function that accepts a configurable issues cap, and let each path pass its own value.

### Proposed changes, file by file

**1. New file: `desloppify/app/commands/review/prompt_sections.py`**

Shared rendering functions extracted from `prompt_template.py`. Contains:
- `_PromptBatchContext` dataclass (moved)
- `_coerce_string_list()` (moved)
- `_build_context()` (moved, renamed to `build_batch_context()` since now public)
- `render_scoring_frame()` (moved, made public)
- `render_scan_evidence_note()` (moved, made public)
- `render_historical_focus(batch)` (moved, made public)
- `render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)` (moved, made public)
- `render_scope_enums()` (moved, made public)
- `_SCAN_EVIDENCE_FOCUS_BY_DIMENSION` and `render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)` (moved)
- `render_package_org_focus(dim_set)`, `render_abstraction_focus(dim_set)`, `render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set)` (moved)
- `render_dimension_focus(context_or_dim_set)` -- overloaded to accept either a `_PromptBatchContext` or a raw `set[str]`
- `render_task_requirements(*, issues_cap: int, dim_set: set[str])` -- extracted core of `_render_task_requirements`, parameterized on issues_cap
- `render_seed_files_block(context)` (moved)
- `render_output_schema(*, batch_name: str | None, batch_index: int | None, dimensions: tuple[str, ...], include_batch_keys: bool, include_session_keys: bool)` -- parameterized to produce either the batch schema or the external session schema

**2. Modified: `desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py`**

Becomes a thin wrapper: imports from `prompt_sections` and composes `render_batch_prompt()` using those building blocks. The batch-specific `_render_metadata_block` stays here. The function signatures and public API (`render_batch_prompt`) remain identical.

**3. Modified: `desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py`**

`_build_claude_launch_prompt` signature changes to:

```python
def _build_claude_launch_prompt(
    *,
    session_id: str,
    token: str,
    blind_path: Path,
    template_path: Path,
    output_path: Path,
    packet: dict[str, Any],
) -> str:
```

The `packet` argument is the already-available packet dict (passed from `do_external_start`, which already has it in scope at line 233). No new data loading needed.

The function body changes to:
1. Session header (session_id, token, blind_path, template_path, output_path) -- same as now
2. `render_scoring_frame()` from prompt_sections
3. `render_scan_evidence_note()` from prompt_sections
4. For each batch in `packet["investigation_batches"]`:
   - Batch header: name, dimensions, rationale, seed files
   - `render_historical_focus(batch)` 
   - `render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)` 
   - `render_dimension_focus(dim_set)` for that batch's dimensions
5. Combined task requirements via `render_task_requirements(issues_cap=total_cap, dim_set=all_dims)` where `total_cap` is sum of per-batch caps or a reasonable combined max
6. `render_scope_enums()` from prompt_sections
7. External output schema (with `session` wrapper, `dimension_notes`, no `batch`/`batch_index` keys)

**4. Modified: `_build_template_payload` in `external.py`**

Add `dimension_notes` to the template:

```python
def _build_template_payload(packet: dict[str, Any], *, session_id: str, token: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    dimensions = [
        dim for dim in packet.get("dimensions", [])
        if isinstance(dim, str) and dim.strip()
    ]
    return {
        "session": {"id": session_id, "token": token},
        "assessments": {dim: 0 for dim in dimensions},
        "dimension_notes": {},
        "issues": [],
    }
```

**5. No changes to `_canonical_external_payload`** -- already passes through all non-session/non-provenance keys including `dimension_notes`.

**6. No changes to the import pipeline** -- `dimension_notes` is already handled as an optional dict.

### Answering your specific questions

**Should the external prompt reuse render functions from prompt_template.py directly?**

No. Extract the shared parts into `prompt_sections.py`. This keeps `prompt_template.py` as a batch-specific composition layer and gives `external.py` access to the same building blocks without importing batch-specific code.

**What shared rendering functions should be extracted?**

All the pure rendering functions listed above. The key insight is that every `_render_*` function in `prompt_template.py` except `_render_metadata_block` is already context-agnostic -- they take either nothing, a dim_set, or a batch dict. They are batch-agnostic building blocks that happen to live in a batch-specific file.

**What is the right function signature for the new `_build_claude_launch_prompt`?**

Add `packet: dict[str, Any]` as a keyword argument. The packet is already in scope at the call site (line 277 of external.py, inside `do_external_start`). No other signature changes needed. The function internally iterates `packet.get("investigation_batches", [])` to render per-batch sections.

**Does the template payload need updating?**

Yes. Add `"dimension_notes": {}` to `_build_template_payload` output. This signals to the reviewer that dimension_notes are expected and gives them the structure to fill in.

**Are there any issues with the submit/import flow accepting dimension_notes from external?**

No. The import pipeline at `importing/parse.py:101` already calls `_coerce_optional_object(payload, key="dimension_notes", errors=errors)` which accepts it as an optional dict defaulting to `{}`. The `_canonical_external_payload` function passes through all keys except `session` and `provenance`. The feedback validation at `parse.py:255-256` already uses `_feedback_dimensions_from_dimension_notes` to credit dimension_notes evidence toward feedback requirements. Everything works end-to-end today if the external reviewer includes dimension_notes.

### Implementation sequence

1. Create `prompt_sections.py` with extracted functions
2. Update `prompt_template.py` to import from `prompt_sections` (keep `render_batch_prompt` API identical)
3. Run tests to verify batch path is unchanged
4. Update `_build_template_payload` to include `dimension_notes: {}`
5. Rewrite `_build_claude_launch_prompt` to accept `packet` and use shared rendering
6. Update the call site in `do_external_start` (line 277) to pass `packet=packet`
7. Update test `test_external_start_creates_session_and_template` to verify the new prompt content (scoring frame present, batch info present, dimension_notes in template)
8. Add a focused test for `_build_claude_launch_prompt` that verifies it includes scoring frame, batch details, seed files, and the full output schema

### Potential challenges

- **Prompt length for external sessions:** If there are many batches (the batch path typically runs 3-5), the combined prompt could be long. This is fine for copy/paste into Claude but worth keeping in mind. The prompt will be roughly N times the per-batch content plus shared sections.

- **Issues cap calculation for external:** Each batch has its own `issues_cap` based on `max_batch_issues_for_dimension_count(len(dims))`. For the combined prompt, use the sum across all batches, or just `max_batch_issues_for_dimension_count(total_dimension_count)`. The latter is cleaner since the external reviewer produces a single combined output.

- **Output schema difference:** The batch schema has `batch` and `batch_index` keys. The external schema has `session.id` and `session.token`. The shared `render_output_schema` needs to be parameterized for this. Alternatively, write the external output schema separately since it is different enough.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py` - Source of all rendering functions to extract
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py` - Primary file to modify: `_build_claude_launch_prompt` and `_build_template_payload`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/parse.py` - Confirms dimension_notes already handled in import pipeline (no changes needed)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/feedback_contract.py` - Shared constants used by both rendering and validation
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/review/import_scoring/test_review_external.py` - Existing test to update for new prompt content and template shape

1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user asked to review and improve the review prompt system used by sub-agent code reviewers in the desloppify tool. The core concerns evolved through the conversation:
   - Initially: Are sub-agents clear that scores should be based on their own code reading, not automated signals?
   - Then: Rethink the scoring philosophy from mechanical (subtract-from-100) to holistic judgment ("how well does this codebase serve a developer from a [dimension] perspective?")
   - Then: Make the prompt succinct without losing context
   - Then: Reconcile the output schema (findings vs issues mismatch between system_prompt and batch prompt)
   - Then: Align the two review paths (batch subagent and external session) so both deliver the same context to reviewers

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Two review paths: **batch path** (codex exec subagents, parallel, per-batch) and **external path** (manual Claude session, single-session, all dimensions)
   - **Blind packet**: JSON file containing system_prompt, dimension_prompts, holistic_context, investigation_batches — shared by both paths, scrubbed of score-anchoring data
   - **Judgment-based scoring**: Scores reflect holistic developer experience, not a formula. Issues illustrate judgment, not inputs to calculation.
   - **Scoring independence**: Automated signals are navigation aids, not evidence or inputs to scores
   - **Two-part frame**: "YOUR TASK" (what to do) + "WHAT WE GIVE YOU AND WHY" (automated signals are navigation aids)
   - **Navigation aid labeling**: Each automated data section gets a brief "navigation aid, not scoring evidence" label instead of per-section caveats
   - **Dimension prompt as rubric**: Each dimension's look_for/skip list defines what good looks like for that specific quality

This quality is reliably strong with no material issues.\n- 90: strong. A developer would trust what they see, with only minor friction.\n- 80: solid but uneven. Recurring friction — moments of \"why is this different?\"\n...\n\nOUTPUT: Return JSON matching the schema in your task instructions. The task prompt specifies the exact output format."

         )
